# Bookworms for iPhone and Mac

Bookworms for iPhone and Mac is a companion to the [Bookworms Apple TV app](https://github.com/ianhyzy/bookworms-tvos/blob/main/README.md). It designs book spines with Apple's Private Cloud Compute or on-device model, or with a model from Anthropic, OpenAI, or Google Gemini when you add your own API key, or from an OpenAI-compatible endpoint with its base URL, model name, and a key if it needs one, and shares them with the Apple TV through iCloud, shows your spines in a stack or a bookcase and your books in 3D scenes on a desk, and makes share images and videos for social media. It never asks for a Hardcover API key, and needs an AI API key only if you choose a third-party provider. Private Cloud Compute needs its managed entitlement in the build's provisioning profile; the test builds that `script/install_on_device.sh --no-pcc` and `script/run_mac.sh --no-pcc` make leave it out (see [Spine design model](#spine-design-model)).

The app targets iOS 27 on iPhone and macOS 27 on Apple silicon Macs (see [On the Mac](#on-the-mac)). It gets your library in one of two ways:

- **From Hardcover.** Sign in to Hardcover on the iPhone or the Mac, and the app reads your Hardcover library, including your reviews. It works without an Apple TV, and unless you keep iCloud off (see [One Hardcover account in iCloud](#one-hardcover-account-in-icloud)), it saves the library to your private iCloud database, so your Apple devices download it from Hardcover about once a day between them. See [Hardcover sign-in](#hardcover-sign-in).
- **From iCloud.** Without a sign-in, the app reads the Hardcover library that your Apple devices saved to the private iCloud container `iCloud.gay.ian.Bookworms`: a signed-in iPhone's or Mac's, or the Apple TV app's, which needs **iCloud storage** turned on in the Apple TV app. When iCloud holds libraries from several Hardcover accounts, the app asks which one to show (see [One Hardcover account in iCloud](#one-hardcover-account-in-icloud)).

Either way, designs are saved to that iCloud container, where the Apple TV picks them up when its **iCloud storage** is on. Until there is a library, the **Share**, **Library**, and **Desk** tabs explain how to get one and offer **Connect Hardcover**, and **View example profile**, a read-only copy of the owner's recent reading that ships in the app (see [No library yet](#no-library-yet) and [Example profile](#example-profile)).

## Features

### Spine generation

The **Spines** tab, on the paper ground, opens on a bindery shelf: as many of the most recently designed spines as fill the window's width, large enough that thin spines letter their authors legibly, newest at the right, which open their books' details. They stand on a bare walnut board that runs to both edges of the window (`FloatingShelfBoard`), with no case or card around it: the shelf heads the **Generation** section rather than filling a row of its own, because the Mac's grouped form draws a card behind a section's rows. A book joins the shelf only once its design is saved: it slides in from the right, pushing the others left and the oldest off the left edge, or under Reduce Motion fades in.

Below the shelf, the **Generation** section shows the run, then a gauge of the library with one sliver per book, the designed books' slivers in their spines' colors, under a count such as "24 of 276 books designed":

- While it runs, a count of the run's books, such as "Designing 12 of 48", stands over a bar. The total covers the whole run. It changes only when the run checks the library again after its work changed, such as when the Apple TV lists more books, and the bar then continues from where it stood, so it never moves backward.
- Under the bar are the book being designed, or with several books in flight the one that started first, and the model designing it, as the **Model** row names it.
- Once the run has timed five books and run for half a minute, the time left follows, such as "About 3 minutes left". It comes from the average time between the latest 40 books' finishes, which is each book's own time when the run designs one book at a time, in whole minutes under 10 minutes, then in 5-minute steps, then in hours. A rise of less than a quarter keeps the last estimate. It is hidden while the run checks the library, pauses, or waits for a retry date.
- A tally follows, such as "6 new designs · 3 from a series · 2 up to date · 1 failed", without its zero entries: new designs from the run's model, new copies of a series' design, and books whose saved design stays, including one moved to the book's new title or author.
- After the run, the section keeps its outcome until the next run, with the time of the last run on the same row, such as "Designed 48 spines in 6 minutes", "Checked 48 books in 2 minutes" when every design stayed, or "Paused until 3:15 PM" ("Paused until Oct 20 at 3:15 PM" on another day) with the reason and the books left.
- VoiceOver reads the run's progress as one element.

Once the library has a design, the **Saved designs** section counts the library's designs by the model that made them (see [Designs from several devices](#designs-from-several-devices)). The **Design model on this iPhone** section, **Design model on this Mac** on the Mac, shows the model tier this device designs with, with an info button that says what a new design costs (see [Spine design model](#spine-design-model)), **Choose model**, and, for Private Cloud Compute, the daily request limit. The tab keeps its details behind info buttons rather than in footers. The **Generation** row's info button (`GenerationInfo`) says when some books have no cover, neither Hardcover's nor one you chose, and draw no design, such as "3 books have no cover, so they get a default style." A run never designs such a book, so it shows a placeholder cover and the default spine cloth. A coverless book in a series can still draw its series' design, so the note leaves it out. Signed in, it says that when the Apple TV has a newer copy of your library, the run designs from it (see step 1 below), and while a background run is in progress, it says so: on the iPhone that run dims **Generate now**, and on the Mac **Stop** ends it. With none of these to say, the row shows no info button. **Generate while charging** (**Generate while idle** on the Mac) explains itself in its own info button, and its footer shows only the Background App Refresh warning. With no library, the shelf stands empty between two bookends with **Connect Hardcover**.

- **Generate now** starts a run in the app. iOS can keep it going after you leave the app through a continued-processing background task, whose system progress display shows the run's count and time left as the **Spines** tab does. iOS ends a continued-processing task whose progress stops changing for some tens of seconds, once the app has left the screen, and one book's design can take longer than that. So while a book is in flight, the reported progress also advances with time, ever more slowly, toward most of the share that the book adds when it finishes (`TaskProgress`). The run tells iOS that the task succeeded however it ends, so a run that you stop shows no failure in the system display; the **Spines** tab says what stopped or failed, and a later run continues the work. When iOS itself ends the task, the app cancels the run, saves it as stopped, and completes the task at once, without waiting for the run to unwind, because iOS may suspend the app first (`GenerationController.continuedTaskExpired(_:)`). Whether that keeps "Task failed" out of the system display is not confirmed on a device. iOS also ends the whole app, and with it the task, when a model request stays open past the system's hold on the app; the request limit described below is meant to prevent that. When a run you started designs at least one book and then pauses until a retry time, such as a provider's rate limit or the daily limit's reset, it starts again in the app at that time, and no sooner than a minute after the pause (`GenerationController.resumeDate(after:now:)`). It does so on battery power and with **Generate while charging** off too, since it continues the run you asked for, until a run designs nothing or the work is done. The Mac starts it while the app runs. The iPhone starts it in the foreground: at that time when the app is open, or else when you next return to the app, if iOS has kept the app in memory and the charging task hasn't run first. After a relaunch, the charging task or **Generate now** continues the work. A run that pauses before it designs a book, as when the model is down, is left to the next background run or **Generate now**, and a run that waits for power resumes when you plug in.
- **Redesign all spines…**, shown once the library has a design, works only with a provider's cloud model, because it exists to replace Apple's models' designs. With Apple chosen under **Choose model**, it says "Choose a cloud model first" and names the providers. Otherwise it asks first, in a confirmation that names the model, such as "Redesign all spines with Google Gemini with your API key?", and says that spines you customized stay and that each spine costs about one request. It then designs anew, with the chosen provider's model, every design made before you confirmed, even Private Cloud Compute's: the provider designs before Private Cloud Compute and ranks with it for this run, as while **Use this model instead of Private Cloud Compute** is on. When the provider can't design, such as without its API key on this device, the run designs as any other would, never redesigns with Apple's models, and says "To redesign all spines, add an API key." Designs with your colors or font stay, and so do series copies; in a series whose books share a look, only the look's source book is designed anew, while you left the look alone, and the other books then copy the remade look. The request lasts on this device until a run has checked the whole library (`GenerationController.redesignsAllBefore`), so a run that pauses or stops, a background run, or **Generate now** after a relaunch finishes the rest. Each book claims its design as on any run, so two devices redesigning at once don't both design one book, and a design another device made after the request counts as done (`SpineWorker.redesignsAll(_:look:bookID:before:)`).
- **Generate while charging** (on by default) asks iOS for a background processing task that needs external power and a network connection, and iOS ends the task when the iPhone is unplugged or when you start using it, often within seconds of the start. iOS chooses when to run it, typically overnight while the iPhone charges, and also whenever a charging iPhone sits idle for a short while. A run that iOS ends may have left work, even one that ended before it counted its books, so the app asks for the next run at once. It needs Background App Refresh; when that is off or restricted, the **Spines** tab says so, and iOS refuses the request. Whenever you leave the app while iOS holds no request, the app asks again, so the task is scheduled once Background App Refresh is on. Background runs download covers only on unmetered networks and stop when Private Cloud Compute's daily limit is almost reached. After a run stops without a retry time, such as one waiting for Wi-Fi or one offline before it loaded its books, the next background run waits until 15 minutes after the stop, and after a run that left no work, until a day after that run, unless new work arrives. A background run doesn't start while a run you started is in progress. With it off, spines are designed only when you ask, with **Generate now** or **Redesign spine**.
- When the Apple TV publishes a new worklist, a silent push schedules the charging task. Generation never runs inside the push's 30-second window. A change to this device's library, after a Hardcover sign-in, sync, or sign-out or an adopted iCloud copy, schedules the charging task the same way, so a new library's first designs don't wait out the day after a run that found no library (`GenerationController.workChanged()`).

Each run designs the books that need it, one at a time with Apple's models and local defaults, and up to eight at once with your backup model (see below):

1. The run picks its account and books, after the iCloud account check clears iCloud for one Hardcover account; otherwise it stops with the check's reason (see [One Hardcover account in iCloud](#one-hardcover-account-in-icloud)). A signed-in iPhone designs only its account's books: its copy of the account's library, unless the Apple TV's worklist of that account is newer than the copy, or the iPhone has no copy. A worklist of the same copy or an older one doesn't decide: fixed covers (see [Hardcover sign-in](#hardcover-sign-in)) give the copy's books the Apple TV's work-item hashes, so their designs match on both. A newer worklist of the account decides, so the books a newer Apple TV copy adds get designs before the iPhone adopts that copy. When the iCloud archive holds a newer snapshot of the worklist's account than the one the worklist lists, such as one that a signed-in iPhone or Mac saved after its own download, the run designs every book of that snapshot until the Apple TV lists it. Without its copy or such a worklist, a signed-in iPhone designs nothing; it never designs another account's books. Signing out stops a run that designs the iPhone's library. Signed out, the iPhone designs the library the check cleared, the only one in the iCloud archive or the one you chose, and a worklist of another account counts as none. Without a real account ID, it uses the legacy `hardcover` placeholder's snapshot; Apple TV builds without the worklist upload only that library. An empty worklist means the Apple TV turned Hardcover off, which stops runs unless the iPhone is signed in.
2. The app downloads the cover over HTTPS (the one you chose, if any; see [Cover choice](#cover-choice)), reads its lettering with Vision, and samples its colors. Vision reads one cover at a time, on a queue of its own, so a book waiting for its read doesn't hold up the other books' downloads, requests, and saves (`CoverTextReader`). A new build's first read can take about 30 seconds while Vision prepares its model, and on a locked Mac a read has never finished. A read that takes longer than 90 seconds (`CoverTextReader.timeout`) is cancelled, and the book fails rather than be designed without the cover's text, so the next day's run tries it again; the run stops, and the books in flight stay queued. Until that read returns, a run that reaches a cover's read stops at once and tries again 15 minutes later (`CompanionError.coverTextTimedOut`, `CompanionError.coverTextBusy`).
3. With Private Cloud Compute or your backup model, the model chooses the title line (step 4); otherwise the app takes the tallest line that shares a word with the title, or else the tallest line. The app crops that line and splits it into ink and the ground behind it. A line set vertically or turned more than 45 degrees is never measured. A tilted line is measured level: its ink turns by the angle of the line's outline from Vision, because a tilted line's upright box is taller than its letters. When more than 55 percent of the crop splits as ink, the margin around Vision's box is nearly as inky as the box, and the swapped split would match some usable face's letter shapes better, ink and ground swap, because the split took the ground for the letters, as when art at the crop's edges shares the letters' color (`TitleLettering.measure(_:line:atlas:)`).
4. Private Cloud Compute and your backup model (see [Spine design model](#spine-design-model)) get one request with the whole cover, the book's title and author, and the lines Vision read, numbered top to bottom with their boxes: up to the 16 tallest lines that can be measured (`TitleLettering.listed(_:in:)`). The model answers which line holds the main title, or none; the title as lettered; its case, which sets the spine in capitals for all or small capitals, as does a measured title line that OCR read mostly in capitals, whatever the model answered; how the letters were made (a typeset font, custom-drawn letters, handwriting, brush or marker, or calligraphy); their Google Fonts category and subclass, such as "Serif / Didone"; up to two Google Fonts themes, such as Art Deco or Woodtype; up to three moods; their weight and width; and a one-sentence note (`BookAnalyzer.readCover`). The app measures the line the model chose. A title on no listed line, such as stacked or curved lettering, isn't measured, so its colors come from the cover's palette, and the model's width and weight answers rank its families instead: "extra condensed" asks for a face narrower than nearly every atlas face's version of the title, "condensed" for one narrower than three quarters of them, and so on, at weight 700 for a bold or black answer (`TitleLettering.rank(_:width:weight:text:penalties:atlas:)`). When a guardrail rejects the whole cover, or the model refuses it (Apple's refusal error, Anthropic's refusal stop reason, a Gemini block or safety finish reason, or an OpenAI-compatible refusal message; `ProviderHTTP.isRefusal(_:provider:)`), the model answers the on-device questions about the crop of Vision's line instead; a neon or pixel and sci-fi answer reads as decorative, so its theme sets the pool whatever the serifs. The on-device model gets one request with three questions about that crop: whether the letters are hand-drawn, which serifs they have, and any special treatment, such as neon outlines, gothic strokes, sci-fi tech, western wood type, or typewriter letters, which the app maps onto the same terms. When a guardrail rejects the crop or the model refuses it, or the on-device model designs and cannot read images, the serif and sans families stay allowed, except rounded ones; themed display, gothic, typewriter, and hand-drawn faces need an answer that names them. What a model reads on the cover decides, and the book's genres never override it. Local defaults send no request and choose families from the book's genres instead.
5. A design model's answers choose families from the whole font atlas: the 113 catalog families and every other Google Fonts family that passes the atlas's filter (`script/spine-font-atlas.swift`), 901 in all. Google's categories, classifications, and tags (`Bookworms/SpineFontTags.json`; see [Credits](#credits)) give each family one identity (`SpineFontTags.identity(of:)`), from the first rule that matches: blackletter, when tagged blackletter without a serif, sans, or slab class, which keeps out mistagged faces such as Bodoni Moda; themed, by its strongest other theme of 50 or more, such as Geo or Tomorrow for Techno and VT323 for Pixel, though Brush doesn't count for handwriting faces; monospace, by Google's category, a monospace tag, or a name ending in Mono; script, when Google files it as handwriting; display, when Google files it as display or its category and classifications mix serif, sans, and script, such as Italiana or Madimi One; otherwise its strongest classification of 50 or more, else the stroke Google records. A family joins only its identity's pools, so a themed, blackletter, monospace, or display face never sets a plain title; the catalog's typeset faces that Google files as display, such as Bebas Neue, count by their class. The first rule that matches decides the pool (`BookAnalyzer.families(for:tags:atlas:)`): a serif or slab title gets serif and slab faces, or a theme's themed faces with serifs, such as Rye for Woodtype, when it has at least three; a blackletter title gets blackletter faces; a script title, or handwritten, brushed, or calligraphic letters, get script faces, while custom-drawn letters follow their category; a monospace or typewriter title gets monospace faces; a theme gets its themed faces, except Distressed, which textured cover art draws for plain lettering, Wacky and Blobby, which models give quirky or busy lettering, and Shaded, a built-in shadow or 3D that the spine can't draw; a sans title gets sans faces; and any other title, such as a decorative one without a theme, gets the serif, slab, and sans faces. Faces tagged playful, cute, childlike, happy, excited, or rounded at 50 or more stay out of the serif, sans, and theme pools unless the reading names one of those moods, and a theme narrows only when at least three faces carry it. Within the pool, the faces tagged with the reading's subclass, such as Didone or Modern for "Serif / Didone", form the shortlist when at least six remain, and the reading's moods settle near ties in the ranking (`BookAnalyzer.moodPenalties(_:for:tags:)`). Without a reading, as for local defaults or a refused crop, the catalog's serif and sans families stay allowed. The app sets the title's text in each family at regular and bold weight from `Bookworms/SpineFontAtlas.lzfse`, small glyph images of every atlas family, and ranks the allowed families by how closely their letter shapes, letter width (ignoring letter spacing), stroke contrast, stroke weight, and the thickness of their horizontal strokes match the cover's. Horizontal strokes, such as crossbars, serifs, and hairlines, tell faces of the same width apart, so a narrow title doesn't simply get the narrowest allowed face. A bold title never gets a face too light to match it: a family whose heaviest version of the title has less than three quarters of the title's ink density is left out, unless no allowed family is that heavy. It measures only the rows of the title line's OCR box, so a picture or line touching the title doesn't count. Each family's title weight, 600 or 700, follows the cover's stroke weight, and a thin title is matched as if it were the atlas's median regular weight: thin strokes read badly at spine size, and many families lack a 500 instance, so 500 would draw their regular. A family with one weight draws that weight. A measurement that no face could produce ranks nothing, so the families come unranked, or ranked by the design model's width and weight answers, as for a title line the atlas can't rank: one whose letters span less than three quarters of the OCR line, as when a two-color title loses one color or OCR reads a gray byline with the title, or one more than 15 percent narrower than every atlas face's version of the text (`TitleLettering.isPlausible(_:composed:)`). The Inline theme, which includes neon outlines, stands only when a plausible measurement's strokes are hairlines, under 60 percent of that median. `script/spine-font-atlas.sh` regenerates the file after the catalog or Google Fonts changes, and leaves it unchanged when a family still fails to download after three tries, and `script/spine-font-tags.py` then regenerates the tags for its families. The atlas filter keeps only families with an open-source license and Latin letters whose glyphs Vision reads, and leaves out faces that lean, have hairline stems, or have broken metrics. Italic, oblique, and slanted faces and Impact are never offered. New designs, local defaults included, also never use faces whose letters lean without declaring a slant (Caveat, Kalam, Permanent Marker, and Shadows Into Light), hairline faces whose stems are under 8 percent of their capitals' height (the ultra-narrow Six Caps and Amatic SC, and the outlined Monoton and Almendra Display), Cinzel Decorative, whose swashes the measurements can't tell from Cinzel, or Italiana, the narrowest serif face, which would win nearly every condensed serif title because the catalog has no condensed serif (`SpineFamily.neverGenerated`).
6. The cloth takes the ground's color, with no more chroma than dyed cloth or paper shows, so nothing is neon. Dark ink goes only on light cloth, never on a mid-tone or saturated one. The title's own color stays when it is a deliberate color that already reads on the cloth; any other ink is near-black or near-white with a trace of its hue (`BookAnalyzer.choice(_:palette:weight:)`). The app downloads the first of up to six ranked families that has every letter of the title and author, at the regular width when the family also ships condensed or expanded files and in the optical size nearest 24 points when it ships several, as the atlas does (`SpineFamily.fileName(for:in:)`), and adjusts the colors in lightness until the text reaches APCA Lc 75, darkening mid-tone cloth under light ink. If none of those families works, the design uses the best match's system typeface. Local defaults also use it when a family can't download, such as when Google Fonts can't be reached, so their run continues; a later run designs the book again once the font can download (see [Spine design model](#spine-design-model)).
7. A book in a series makes no request (see [Series designs](#series-designs)): a book whose series has a look saves a copy of the look, whichever device made it, and a book in a series without a look reuses the saved design of the series' source, unless that design names a blocked font, such as Impact, which the run never copies. When the font lacks a glyph of the book's title or author, the copy letters in the font's system typeface, as the series then draws. Only a book ranked before it can be a source, and the run designs each series' books in rank order, so a source is always designed from its own cover. With local defaults, a series font that can't download, such as while Google Fonts can't be reached, gives the book a design of its own in its system typeface, so the run goes on.
8. The app saves a `SpineDesignV1` record with generator version `ios-pcc-v2` at every tier and records the tier in `provenance` (see [iCloud contract](#icloud-contract)). When the run designed the book from its cover, rather than copying a look or another book's design, and the book's series has no look yet, the app creates the look from it. When another device saved a design of the book meanwhile that the new design doesn't replace (see [iCloud contract](#icloud-contract)), iCloud keeps that design, and the run shows it instead and creates no look from its own.

With your backup model (see [Spine design model](#spine-design-model)), a run designs up to eight books at once (`SpineWorker.backupConcurrency`), because most of a book's time is the wait for the provider's answer. Private Cloud Compute, the on-device model, and local defaults design one book at a time. The books of one series look group take turns in rank order, so the group's first design decides its look before the next book reads it. The run saves the finished books one at a time, in the order they finish, and the count, the time left, and the tally count each book once. A stop from any book, such as the provider's rate limit, a Google Fonts or iCloud outage, or **Stop**, stops the whole run once: the run cancels the other books in flight, including a cover's text read, and saves nothing from them, so they stay queued for the next run. A slow book holds only its own place, and the run starts the next book as soon as any book finishes. A book that fails on its own, such as one with an unreadable cover, still fails alone, and the run goes on. Books that need the same cover, or the same Google Fonts family, share one download of it.

A new design sends one request to the run's model, and a second only when a guardrail or the model rejects the whole cover. Local defaults send none. Before a model's request, the run claims the book in iCloud (see [iCloud contract](#icloud-contract)), so an iPhone and a Mac that run at the same time don't each spend a request on it: the device that finds another device's claim from the last 10 minutes leaves the book out of its run, and its next run keeps the design that device saved, or designs the book if none was. The reader's **Redesign spine** and a design of the cover the reader chose claim nothing and always send their request. The app rechecks a book when its metadata or its chosen cover changes, when the book's last check on this device ran with Apple's on-device model or default styling and the run's model tier is better, when Apple's model designed the book in place of your model and your model runs again (see [Spine design model](#spine-design-model)), or after seven days. A new generator version alone rechecks no book. A build installed over one that uses the other iCloud environment, such as a TestFlight build over a development build, checks every book again, because each environment has its own database (`CloudEnvironment`, from the iPhone's provisioning profile and the Mac's signed `icloud-container-environment` entitlement). Each check reads the book's saved design from iCloud before it downloads the cover. When a book shows its default cover and its cover choice is unchanged since its last check on this device, or the book has no check on this device yet, a saved design that the run keeps stays, or moves to the book's new metadata, without a download, so the weekly recheck of a current spine downloads no cover. A cover you chose is downloaded at every check, and a saved design with the generator's colors that doesn't come from it, by its cover hash, is designed again from it at the run's tier, whatever tier made the saved design: a device that never saw your choice, or whose first design from it failed, still designs your cover, and so does a new image behind its URL. When your chosen cover is missing and the default cover downloads instead, the saved design stays. A design keeps the hash of the cover it comes from: a new image behind the default cover, such as one Hardcover replaced, changes no design. The one exception is a sharper default cover: a sync changes a book's default cover only to replace a cover under 1,000 pixels tall with one at least that tall (see [Hardcover sign-in](#hardcover-sign-in)), so when the book's default cover URL changed since its last successful check on this device, the run downloads the default cover, and a book's own design, not a series copy, with the generator's colors and font that comes from another image is designed again from the new cover at the run's tier, whatever tier made it, unless the book draws its series' look. A design whose colors or font you chose stays, also when you choose them while the new cover downloads or designs. When the new cover is missing or unreadable, the design moves to the new metadata as below; a network or server error fails the book, and the design stays under its old metadata until a later check downloads the cover. A device whose ledger has no default cover for the book, such as one that never checked it, treats the cover as unchanged. So a design that another device made from a cover you chose there and later removed stays until a device whose last check of the book saw that cover checks it again and designs it from the default cover. A run replaces a saved design only when it comes from Apple's on-device model or default styling and the run's model tier is better, or when Private Cloud Compute made it on this device in place of your model, which couldn't answer. A design from Private Cloud Compute or your model with an API key stays whichever model runs, and so does a design from the run's tier or a better one, whichever generator version made it. So the first run with a better model starts redesigning the spines that the on-device model or default styling made, no run replaces a capable model's spines, and a run at the same or a weaker tier never replaces a model's choices. A run checks new books and books whose metadata or chosen cover changed before the books it rechecks only to upgrade, so Private Cloud Compute's daily limit, spent on upgrades, doesn't hold back a new book's first spine. When the book's metadata changed since the design, such as after a title edit, the run saves the design that stays under the book's new work-item hash, with its cover hash, colors, font, generator, and date, so the Apple TV still draws it, provided its font letters the book's title and author. Otherwise the design stays under its old work-item hash: the iPhone and Mac still draw it on its book, and the Apple TV draws its own local spine for the book until a better tier or the redesign button designs it again. A copy of a series look or of another book's design replaces a book's saved design under the same rule, unless you chose the book's cover; otherwise the run designs the book at its tier. A run that would stop for its model, such as at Private Cloud Compute's daily limit or, in the background, near it, queues no such upgrades; when upgrades are all that's left, it still pauses until the reset, as a run with books queued does. The daily limit also stops a run partway through upgrades until the reset. When the backup model's lasting rejection moves a run to another tier (see [Spine design model](#spine-design-model)), the run skips the books it queued only to upgrade to the backup. A book that draws its series' look keeps its saved design, whichever generator or tier made it, and saves it under a new work-item hash in the same way, except a copy of an older look with the generator's colors, such as one made before a better tier upgraded the look or before you redesigned or recolored it: the run saves a new copy of the look as it is now in its place, with no request and no cover download. A book draws its series' look whatever the look's font letters (see [Series designs](#series-designs)). The redesign button on a book's details, **Redesign spine** or, in a series that shares a look or design, **Redesign spines**, starts a run that designs the book first, or the book its series' spine comes from, even when that design is current, but never over a design or look from a better tier than the run's unless you chose another cover since (see [Series designs](#series-designs)). While **Use this model instead of Private Cloud Compute** is on, your model ranks with Private Cloud Compute for a redesign, so its redesign replaces Private Cloud Compute's designs and looks too. That run leaves the spine as it is and says which model made it. When the backup model's lasting rejection moves the run to another tier during the redesign, the run checks the new design's tier the same way and shows the rejection instead. A recheck keeps a spine whose colors or font you chose until you choose another cover (see [Spine colors](#spine-colors) and [Spine font](#spine-font)). A book that fails waits 24 hours unless its metadata or chosen cover changes. Quota, network, service, Google Fonts, and iCloud save errors stop the run and schedule the next attempt for the reset or retry time, except that a Google Fonts failure under local defaults gives the new design a system typeface instead, and the app checks that book again after a day. A font request whose connection drops or times out, or that gets a server error, is sent again after about 1, 2, and then 4 seconds (`GoogleFonts.retrying`); a rate limit or a fourth failure counts as an outage. A Google Fonts outage waits for its `Retry-After` time, at most one day, or 15 minutes without one. Full iCloud storage pauses background runs for a day; **Generate now** runs anyway. A model tier that becomes unavailable during a run stops it without a retry date, and the next run designs with the best tier available then. So does a request the model can't take at all, such as one that needs a capability the model lacks, which would otherwise fail every book alike and make each wait 24 hours.

Each failed model request writes its error's domain and code to the `generation` log (subsystem `gay.ian.Bookworms.iOS`), which TestFlight builds show. When Apple's model framework pauses the run, such as after "The design model is busy or unreachable," the line also carries Apple's description of the failure. A request to Private Cloud Compute or the on-device model that gets no answer within 20 seconds is cancelled and sent once more (`BookAnalyzer.answer(within:tries:_:)`). When the second gets no answer either, the book fails and the run pauses with that message and a retry time 15 minutes later (`CompanionError.modelTimedOut`), so the next run moves past the book and tries it again after a day. The limit is that short because Apple's model framework holds a system assertion on the app for each request ("Finish Task Uninterruptable" in the system log), which expires after about 30 seconds: iOS ends an app in the background that still holds it then, and the run's background task shows "Task failed". The limit is meant to cancel the request in time for the framework to release the assertion; whether it does has not been confirmed on a device. When that model was standing in for an unreachable backup model, only the book fails and the run continues. A backup model's own requests keep their five-minute limit. One such pause is the system's per-app model rate limit, which iOS and macOS lift while the device charges: a large run on battery hits it, and Spines shows "Paused until plugged in" (`CompanionError.batteryRateLimited`). Plugging in resumes the run at once while the app runs, and opening the app on power does too, with no notification. Otherwise the run tries again at the reset time the system gives, or after 15 minutes, which a suspended iPhone app does in the charging task. Other failures' descriptions, such as a backup provider's, can quote book content, so they stay private. Read the log with Console.app while the device is connected, or afterward with `sudo log collect --device-udid <UDID> --start "<time>"` and `log show --predicate 'subsystem == "gay.ian.Bookworms.iOS" AND category == "generation"'` on the archive.

### Series designs

Books in one series draw with one spine look: the same font, colors, case, and weight, with only the title and thickness changing. Whichever device designs a series first, this app or the Apple TV app, decides its look, unless it comes from Apple's on-device model or default styling and a better model tier upgrades it, and the app draws it in every view and share image. `Bookworms/Models/SeriesDesigns.swift` holds the grouping rules; the Apple TV app mirrors `styleSeries`, `lookKey`, and `subseries`.

- **Style series.** Hardcover can list a book in several series, and the app downloads all of them (`CloudBook.seriesMemberships`). A book takes its look from its shortest series, so a Discworld book that also belongs to a smaller series looks like that series' books instead of like every Discworld book. Compilations count last, then series by their number of books, with one-book series and series of unknown size last, then Hardcover's featured series, then the lower series ID. A book without memberships, such as one from an Apple TV archive saved before they existed, uses its featured series. The book's **Series** choice can override this (see below). `SeriesDesigns.styleSeries(of:link:)` is the rule.
- **Look groups.** Books share a look when they have the same style series and the same first author, compared without case, diacritics, spaces, or punctuation (`SeriesDesigns.lookKey(of:link:)`). Long series that Hardcover doesn't split can be grouped by title in `SeriesDesigns.subseries`: when Discworld is the style series, books share a look only within their reading-order group, such as City Watch, Witches, or Rincewind, and a Discworld book that no group names keeps its own design. The Apple TV app keeps a copy of the same table.
- **The first look decides.** A group's look is a `SeriesLookV1` record in iCloud (see [iCloud contract](#icloud-contract)). A run that designs a book from its cover, at any tier, creates the look of the book's group from that design when the group has none. After each reload that loads the library, the app also creates the look of each group that has none and has a book with a usable saved design (an allowed font, never Impact or an italic) made for the book's current title, author, and covers, as its work-item hash shows: from the book whose design was made earliest, preferring designs that are not copies of another book's (`SeriesDesigns.missingLooks`). Choosing a book's series runs the same check, but neither the launch cache nor the library that a failed reload leaves on screen seeds a look. A device creates only a missing look, so among looks from one model tier the first one saved decides. A run whose tier is better than a look's when the look comes from Apple's on-device model or default styling (see [Spine design model](#spine-design-model)), or a run at the look's tier when the look awaits a font that couldn't download, replaces a look the reader never edited (`SeriesLookV1.isUpgraded(at:awaitingFont:)`): it designs the look's source book, or, for an Apple TV look or a source book that left the group, the group's best-ranked book with a cover (`SeriesLookV1.upgradeSource(among:)`), and makes the look again from that design with the look's creation date. When that book's own saved design stays, such as one from the run's tier or better or from a model with an API key, the look takes it without a request, and so does a copy of a look or of another book's design that records its source's model tier. A copy without a model token, made before copies recorded one, is designed again instead and replaced at the run's tier, because it says nothing about its source's tier. When another device first creates a group's look from Apple's on-device model or default styling, a run at a better tier upgrades it the same way at once. A look from Private Cloud Compute or a model with an API key stays whichever model runs, except a look made from a stand-in that your model designs again (see [Spine design model](#spine-design-model)): when the group's look still exactly matches a copy of the stand-in that the reader never edited, as the catch-up makes one and an upgrade from a check that keeps the stand-in makes one (`SpineWorker.isLook(_:madeFrom:)`), the redo designs the book as if the group had no look, whatever the look's tier. The new design then makes the look again, unedited, when it comes from your model, or from the run's next tier after a lasting rejection when that tier is the stand-in's or better, provided the look is still the one the redo read; a design from a lower tier, such as one made after a lasting rejection moved the run to the on-device model, and a new stand-in, such as Private Cloud Compute's in place of the on-device model's while your model still can't answer, leave the look. A look that doesn't match, such as one the reader edited or redesigned, or one made from another design, stays, and the redo copies it. The group's other books keep drawing the look and send no request, and the same run saves their copies of the old look again from the new look (`SpineWorker.refreshCopies(of:besides:)`); so does a redesign that remakes the look. A look the reader recolored, set in another font, or redesigned, or one made from a design whose colors or font the reader chose, is never replaced this way.
- **Drawing a look.** Every book in a group with a look draws it, with its own lettering, cover, thickness, and bands, unless its **Changes apply to** choice is **This book only**. A look from the Apple TV can name a system face without a Google Fonts family, such as Georgia-Bold; the app draws it in that face when the device has it, and otherwise in the look's system typeface.
- **Before a group has a look**, for example offline, with iCloud off, or while CloudKit rejects the record, the app draws the group by rank. It ranks the group's books by position in the style series, then earliest finish, then book ID; a missing position or finish ranks last, so reading the first book or a prequel later moves the group to its design. In that order, every book after the group's best-ranked book with a saved design made for that book's current title, author, and covers, as its work-item hash shows, draws that book's design (`SeriesDesigns.sources(ranked:designs:)`); earlier books draw their own. A design made for a book's earlier metadata draws only that book. A design saved in a blocked font, such as Impact, lends the allowed upright font readers draw it in, such as Bebas Neue.
- **One font per series.** The books that draw one look or one source's design letter it in its font only when the downloaded font has glyphs for the title and author of every one of them, in capitals when the look or design uses them. Otherwise all of them letter it in its system typeface (`SpineDesignV1.inSystemTypeface`), which letters any book, with the look's or design's colors, weight, and case; no book leaves its series' design, or takes another book's, because the font lacks a glyph. Until the font loads, the books draw it as usual. The app checks once per face and lettering whenever a face loads, on any screen, never while drawing (`AppModel.alignSeries()`). The Apple TV applies the same rule.

On a book's details, the **Spine design** card at the end of the page shows the book's series choices, under the spine:

- **Series** shows when the book has a series or a series choice. It offers **Default**, labeled with the shortest series' name, such as "Default — Discworld"; each of the book's series, with its number of books when Hardcover records it, such as "Discworld (41 books)"; and **Not in a series**. Choosing the shortest series by name saves the same choice as **Default**, so the book keeps following Hardcover's series. A choice that Hardcover no longer lists for the book shows as **Linked series**. The **Series** row of a book's **Details** card, or its **Series** card with glass cards, names the same series. Its **Series** list picks among the book's Hardcover series with check buttons, keeping **Changes apply to**; it offers no **Not in a series** or **Linked series** choice, and a book out of every series shows no **Series** row or card (see [Library](#library)).
- **Changes apply to** shows when the book's look group has a look or another book in the library. **Whole series** (the default) makes the cover, the colors, their resets, and the redesign change the whole series. While other books draw the same look or design, the buttons read **Change series cover** and **Redesign spines**; a book alone in its group keeps **Change cover** and **Redesign spine**, and its edits still change the look. **This book only** makes the book draw its own design, and those edits then change only it.
- Each row stands beside its label when both fit on one line, and under it otherwise, so a long series name such as "Default — Discworld - Ankh-Morpork City Watch" wraps under **Series** without overlapping the next row. At accessibility text sizes, where the two segments don't fit, **Changes apply to** becomes a menu.
- Each choice shows at once and saves as the book's `SeriesLinkV1` record in iCloud (see [iCloud contract](#icloud-contract)), so other devices and generation runs follow it. Choosing **Default** with **Series** deletes the record. Both rows take no other choice until the save ends. If the save fails, a note under the rows says "Couldn't save the series choice.", followed by the reason, such as "Sign in to iCloud on this iPhone." or "Bookworms couldn't reach iCloud. Check your connection and try again." (`SpineWorker.iCloudMessage(for:)`), and the saved choice returns; the note stays until a later choice saves. A check in **Series** that fails to save shows the reason in an alert, "The series choice was not changed", which shows over the sheet on the Mac too.

With **Changes apply to** set to **Whole series**, the edits act on the series:

- **Change series cover** chooses the cover of the book the series' look or design was made from while that book draws it too, and otherwise this book's cover.
- Colors and fonts go to the look, or, in a group without a look, to the source's design (see [Spine colors](#spine-colors) and [Spine font](#spine-font)).
- The redesign button, **Redesign spines**, or **Redesign spine** for a book alone in its group, makes a new design for that same book, from its cover, even when its design is current, and the look then takes the new design's colors and type. In a group without a look, the new design becomes the look. A redesign never replaces a design or look from a better model than this device's, except that your model's redesign replaces Private Cloud Compute's while your model overrides it (see [Designs from several devices](#designs-from-several-devices)).

### Hardcover sign-in

Outside preview mode, the app opens the Hardcover sheet over whichever tab shows at the first launch on a device that is not signed in, which it records (`BookwormsOfferedHardcover` in user defaults), and at once when Hardcover rejects a working login; from then on, the iPhone's bar and the Mac's sidebar say so (see [Where the account shows](#where-the-account-shows)). Otherwise, **Settings**, a gear in the **Spines** tab's toolbar, opens it on the iPhone, the sidebar's account row does on the Mac, and so does **Connect Hardcover** on the **Share**, **Library**, and **Desk** tabs when there is no library; on the Mac, **Settings > Hardcover** shows the same account in place. The sheet is titled **Hardcover** and holds only the account, except that the iPhone's gear opens it as **Settings**, where, signed in or not, **Book details** follows the account (see [Library](#library)), then **Privacy Policy**, which opens https://ian.gay/bookworms-privacy-policy/, and **Credits**, which shows the attributions in `Bookworms/Credits.rtf` (see [Credits](#credits)), and the sheet still opens at the top, on the account. Signed out, it says in one line what Bookworms does, under the two bookworms from the app's icon as line art, explains what signing in adds (your reading history, your reviews, and read-only access), and offers a prominent **Connect Hardcover**, the default action (Return on the Mac), and **Not now**, the cancel action (Escape). Under **Connect Hardcover**, **View example profile** closes the sheet and opens the [example profile](#example-profile) while the device has no library, and **Leave example profile** takes its place while the example shows. Below them, **iCloud library** shows when the library in iCloud last synced, or that there is none yet, any error from loading it, and **Sync now**, which loads the iCloud library again; the example profile hides it. Preview mode shows the preview library's **Last synced** there instead, under **Preview library**, where **Sync now** loads the preview folder again. Sign-in uses the same OAuth 2.0 Device Authorization Grant, client ID, read-only scopes, and endpoints (`https://api.hardcover.app/oauth2/device` and `https://api.hardcover.app/oauth2/token`) as the Apple TV app (see the [Apple TV authentication guide](https://github.com/ianhyzy/bookworms-tvos/blob/main/docs/HARDCOVER_AUTHENTICATION.md)):

1. The app requests a code from Hardcover and shows it typed large on a library card, with **Copy code**.
2. **Open Hardcover** copies the code and opens Hardcover's approval page in Safari over the sheet: the link with the code filled in when Hardcover provides one, otherwise `hardcover.app/link`. Sign in to Hardcover if asked, enter the code, and allow Bookworms.
3. The app polls Hardcover at the interval it asks for and waits 5 seconds longer after each `slow_down` or HTTP 429. It keeps polling through network and server errors, and stops when the code expires or the request is declined; **Get a new code** starts over. Safari closes on its own once the app sees the approval, or when the sign-in ends another way.
4. Once approved, the app checks the account with a `me` query, then saves the login: the access token, the refresh token, and when the access token expires.

Hardcover's access tokens last a week, and its refresh tokens last six months and rotate on every use. The app renews the login with its refresh token before a request when the access token expires within an hour, and when Hardcover rejects the access token, it renews the login once and repeats the request. Requests that need a new token at the same time wait for one renewal, and the renewal request is never retried, because Hardcover revokes the whole login when a spent refresh token comes back. The app saves the new access and refresh tokens; on the iPhone, it asks iOS for time to finish a renewal when you leave the app, because Hardcover spends the refresh token as soon as the request arrives. Hardcover rejects the refresh token only when it answers the renewal with HTTP 400 or 401 and the OAuth error `invalid_grant`; the Apple TV and Android apps use the same rule. Any other reply, such as another OAuth error, a rate limit, a server error, or another 4xx status, and a network failure keep the login for the next request, and until the access token expires, requests keep using it. When the new tokens can't be saved, the app uses them from memory, so it doesn't send the spent refresh token again while it runs, and tries to save them again at **Sync now** and, on the iPhone, when the app becomes active. If the app quits before a save succeeds, the next launch renews with the spent refresh token, Hardcover revokes the login, and the app asks you to reconnect. A login saved by an earlier build has no refresh token and lasts until Hardcover rejects it.

The signed-in sheet shows an "Ex libris" bookplate with your Hardcover photo, once the Share tab's friends list has loaded it, your username, and when the library last synced, or during the first download, **Downloading** with the books so far, then **Sync now** and **Sign out**. When Hardcover rejects the saved login (HTTP 400 or 401 with the OAuth error `invalid_grant` for the refresh token, HTTP 401 for a login without one, or HTTP 403 with `insufficient_scope`), the sheet says so and offers **Reconnect**, and the app says so outside the sheet too (see [Where the account shows](#where-the-account-shows)); the copy of the library stays until then, and reconnecting the same account keeps it and its sync date. Other 403 responses are reported as denied requests, not as an expired login. The sheet also shows other download and load errors, such as a failed design load from iCloud, which offline reads "Bookworms couldn't reach iCloud. Check your connection and try again.", and the iPhone's gear shows a warning badge and the Mac's account row a red mark, even while the app shows an older copy of the library. Signed in without an iCloud account, with iCloud restricted, or with iCloud storage turned off, the library shows without designs or choices; while iCloud holds another Hardcover account's data, it keeps only the designs and choices already on screen (see [One Hardcover account in iCloud](#one-hardcover-account-in-icloud)). The sheet says "Sign in to iCloud on this iPhone.", "iCloud access is restricted on this iPhone.", "iCloud has data from another Hardcover account. Choose Merge or Keep iCloud Off.", or "iCloud storage is off on this iPhone." (on the Mac, "this Mac"), its footer says the copy stays on this device without naming iCloud, and the gear or the account row shows the same sign. With iCloud storage off, the sheet also offers **Turn on iCloud storage**, signed in or not (see [One Hardcover account in iCloud](#one-hardcover-account-in-icloud)).

While signed in:

- The Share, Library, and Desk tabs show the Hardcover account's own library: every book on its shelves except those marked **Ignored**, which the Apple TV leaves out too, with the same covers, descriptions, page counts, finish and start dates, ratings, community ratings and their distribution by half star, formats, genres, series, and progress as the Apple TV builds. Each book also carries the date of every read you finished, newest first (`CloudBook.finishedDates`, from every finished `user_book_reads` row, in the same request as the rest of the book), your review as plain text (`CloudBook.review`, from Hardcover's `user_books.review` with HTML and Markdown removed), its Hardcover shelf (`CloudBook.statusID`, from `user_books.status_id`), which Book Swap reads, its edition's format (`CloudBook.editionFormat`, from Hardcover's `edition_format`), which no view draws (see [Desk scene](#desk-scene)), its featured series' name (`CloudBook.seriesName`), which share images use to shorten titles, and every series it belongs to (`CloudBook.seriesMemberships`), which decides its series look (see [Series designs](#series-designs)). The Apple TV's archive also carries the series name, except in archives saved before the Apple TV fetched names. Designs for these books still come from iCloud, matched by the same account ID the Apple TV uses (the Hardcover user ID).
- No device owns the library. Each Apple device that downloads it from Hardcover saves it to your private iCloud database as the account's snapshot in the `library-v1` archive, and the others use that copy (see [iCloud contract](#icloud-contract)); a device where iCloud is paused or off saves only its own copy (see [One Hardcover account in iCloud](#one-hardcover-account-in-icloud)). When the app launches or becomes active, and on every reload the app or **Try again** starts, the app reads the archive before every download from Hardcover that is due (see below), and otherwise when the iPhone has no copy, or when its copy is at least four hours old, at most once every four hours while the app runs. It adopts the account's snapshot there when it is newer. A book in that snapshot without `finishedDates`, as an Apple TV build that predates the list saves it, or with an empty list, keeps the iPhone's list when the iPhone's newest finish of it is the snapshot's `finished`; otherwise it shows one read until the iPhone downloads the library again. **Last synced** then shows when that copy was downloaded. Adopting needs no Hardcover login, so it also works while the sheet asks you to **Reconnect**.
- The app downloads the library from Hardcover only when the newest copy, on the iPhone or in iCloud, is at least 24 hours old, and whenever you choose **Sync now**. When iCloud is unavailable, the iPhone's copy alone decides. After each download, it saves the library, with your reviews and the books' series, into the archive, replacing only this account's Hardcover snapshot, and only when its own is newer. A failed upload is logged and dropped; the other devices then download the library themselves. When the archive can't be read and the iPhone has no copy, the download is not uploaded, because its books didn't keep the covers of the account's last copy.
- A download, including **Sync now**, keeps each book's covers from the account's newest copy, on the iPhone or in iCloud, so designs and cached covers keep matching on every device, also when you change the book's edition on Hardcover; **Change cover** shows another (see [Cover choice](#cover-choice)). A book takes this download's covers when it is new, when its last copy had no preferred cover, or when its saved preferred cover is among this download's candidates with a reported height below 1,000 pixels and the new preferred cover reports at least 1,000 pixels. A cover without a reported size never replaces another and is never replaced. The Apple TV app applies the same rule.
- A download also reads every series the books belong to, each featured series and each of `seriesMemberships`, 50 per request, paced like library pages, for book details in this app and on the Apple TV. If that fails, the library still saves, with the series of the account's newest copy, on the iPhone or in iCloud; when that copy has none, the archive keeps the series it had for the account.
- Each Hardcover query, including the account check and the library download, retries server errors, timeouts, and dropped connections up to twice, after about 2 and then 4 seconds, and retries a rate limit whose `Retry-After` is 30 seconds or less. A query that still times out after its retries, or a sign-in or renewal request that times out, says "Hardcover did not respond in time. Check your network and try again." A sign-in that waits for your approval keeps checking through timeouts.
- A signed-in iPhone never uses another account's snapshot in the archive. Until the iPhone has its first copy, downloaded or adopted, the app shows no library, or an earlier copy only when it belongs to the same account, and a generation run reports "Waiting for your Hardcover library to download". If the download fails, or iOS deleted the copy and the iPhone is offline, the library stays empty, and the **Share**, **Library**, and **Desk** tabs and the Hardcover sheet show the error (see [No library yet](#no-library-yet)).

- The Share tab's **With friends** templates also read the people the account follows and readers you look up by username (see [Compare with friends](#compare-with-friends)).

**Sign out** stops a renewal in progress and deletes the login, the copy of the library, and the friends' ratings from the device, on the iPhone and on the Mac. It also empties the image cache, in memory and on disk, which holds covers and the friends' profile photos (`CoverStore.clear()`); an image that is still downloading reaches the view that asked for it and isn't kept. A sign-in to another account forgets the friends and the images the same way. The snapshot it saved to iCloud stays, and the covers the signed-out app shows download again, so the signed-out app shows the archive's library, which can be the one this iPhone saved, with your reviews; when the archive holds several accounts' libraries, it shows the one you choose. It also asks Hardcover to revoke the login (`https://api.hardcover.app/oauth2/revoke`), as the Apple TV app does, so Hardcover's Authorized Apps page stops listing Bookworms; the request is sent once, and a failure is ignored. Your library and reading history at Hardcover are unchanged. Preview mode (`-BookwormsPreviewFolder`) ignores the sign-in.

#### Where the account shows

The app says when this device doesn't sync (`AccountControl` and `AccountBanner` in `AccountControl.swift`):

| Sign-in | iPhone | Mac |
|---|---|---|
| None on this device, even while the library shows from iCloud | A **Connect Hardcover** button beside the gear in the **Spines** tab's toolbar; opens the sheet | The sidebar's account row reads **Connect Hardcover**; opens the sheet |
| Rejected by Hardcover | A **Reconnect Hardcover** button beside the gear, and a bar above Share, Library, and Desk with "Not syncing" and **Reconnect Hardcover**; both open the sheet on a new sign-in code | A red mark on the account row's photo; the row opens the sheet on a new sign-in code |
| Working | The gear alone, with a warning badge for any other download or load error | The account row, with the red mark for any other download or load error |

On the iPhone, the words replace the badge, so the gear shows none beside them. The bar is the example profile's: dark above Library and Desk, to match their backdrop, and in the system's appearance above Share. A device with no sign-in shows no bar.

The Mac's account row (`SidebarAccount`) is the sidebar's header, above **Share** in every section, as the Apple TV's sidebar shows the reader: the Hardcover photo, once the Share section's friends list has loaded it, else a person symbol, and the username, on two lines while the sidebar is too narrow for one. Selecting the row opens the Hardcover sheet. The row takes the place of a toolbar button and of the bar, so with the sidebar hidden the Mac shows neither; the menu bar's **Library > Hardcover Account…** still opens the sheet. Without a username, as in preview mode, the row reads "Hardcover account".

Preview mode ignores the sign-in and shows neither the words nor the bar. The app saves a rejected login (`HardcoverNeedsReconnect` in user defaults), so these signs show from the next launch, before any request, until a sign-in or a sign-out.

#### Data on the iPhone

- The login (the access token, the refresh token, and the access token's expiry) is one Keychain item that is readable after the first unlock, never synchronizable, and limited to this device. The app reads it at launch and keeps the login in memory, so requests don't read the Keychain. The app sends the tokens only to Hardcover. Builds without refresh tokens read only a bare access token from that item, so after this build saves a login, such a build asks you to reconnect, and the login it saves has no refresh token.
- The library, including reviews, is a file in `Caches/Bookworms`, which iOS does not back up and may delete; the app then adopts the iCloud copy or downloads it again. The same library is the account's snapshot in the `library-v1` archive in your private iCloud database, which only your Apple Account can read and which signing out leaves in place. The username, account ID, and last sync date are in the app's user defaults. They mark the sign-in, so the sheet and the library source never depend on the cached file.
- A reinstalled app, or a backup restored to another iPhone, starts signed out. The Keychain keeps the login after the app is deleted, and a backup carries the user defaults but not the device-only login, so at launch the app deletes either half without the other.
- When the Keychain refuses to read the login, as before the first unlock after a restart, the app deletes nothing. The saved account keeps the sign-in, and syncs and requests report that the login couldn't be read without asking the Keychain again. **Sync now** reads the login again. The app also reads it when it becomes active. When the user defaults can't be read either, as before the first unlock, the app starts signed out and deletes nothing. If a later read in the same launch finds the login but still no saved account, the app keeps the login and stays signed out, and the next launch restores or deletes it.
- On a Mac, the login and API keys live in the data protection keychain, as on the iPhone (`KeychainSecret`), so the app's bundle ID and team grant access, not its code signature, and the Mac never asks for the login keychain password. Each bundle ID keeps its own items: the installed development copy (`gay.ian.Bookworms.Mac`) and the store copy (`gay.ian.Bookworms.iOS`, which TestFlight, the App Store, and Xcode builds use) never see each other's login or keys. A copy signed ad hoc, such as the visual check's, can't use the Keychain at all, so it has no login or keys.
- A preview launch (`-BookwormsPreviewFolder`), the Debug stub sign-in (`-BookwormsStubSignIn`), and unit tests keep a login, account summary, and library copy of their own: the Keychain service `gay.ian.Bookworms.iOS.hardcover.scratch`, the user defaults suite `gay.ian.Bookworms.scratch`, and `Caches/Bookworms/Scratch`. They never read, prompt for, or delete your login. Other Debug launch arguments, such as `-BookwormsStubCovers` and `-BookwormsShareFriends`, use your login unless you also pass `-BookwormsPreviewFolder`.
- The profiles, names, photo links, and ratings of the readers you follow or looked up are a file in `Caches/Bookworms` (`social-snapshot.json`), with the same eviction as the library. It holds no reviews. Signing out, or signing in to another account, deletes it.
- The app only reads your library from Hardcover and sends no mutations. **Sign out** revokes only the app's own login.

### No library yet

Without a library, the **Share**, **Library**, and **Desk** tabs say why, from the Hardcover sign-in and the last load (`LibraryStatus`), under a drawing of the tab's empty room: **Library** an empty bookcase with two brass bookends, **Desk** a still of the empty desk under the lamp, baked once (`DeskStill`, from `script/room-textures.swift`) so no 3D scene runs, and **Share** a blotter with a blank card. The main action in each state is a prominent button:

- **Signed out:** **No library yet** explains how to get one: connect Hardcover on this iPhone or on another of your Apple devices, or turn on **iCloud storage** in the Bookworms app on the Apple TV. Below them, it shows any error from loading the iCloud library after **iCloud library:**, such as "Sign in to iCloud with the account your other Apple devices use." for a missing iCloud account, or "iCloud access is restricted on this iPhone." when Screen Time or device management restricts it. It offers **Connect Hardcover**, which opens the Hardcover sheet, **View example profile**, which opens the [example profile](#example-profile) in builds that include it, and **Try again**, which reloads.
- **Signed in, downloading:** the Hardcover library is downloading. While this device has no copy of the library, the download first asks Hardcover how many books the account has, then shows a bar with the books so far, such as "212 of 412 books", or the count alone when Hardcover doesn't say; on **Library**, plain-cloth books slide onto the empty shelf as the pages arrive, and appear in place with Reduce Motion on.
- **Signed in, download failed:** the error, such as Hardcover being unavailable, with a prominent **Try again**.
- **Signed in, login rejected:** **Reconnect Hardcover** with the error and **Reconnect**, which opens the Hardcover sheet and starts a sign-in, or shows the code of a sign-in already in progress.

The sheet stays open when the library arrives. Preview mode ignores the sign-in, so it offers only **Try again**. A library without books says that books appear after one of your Apple devices next syncs with Hardcover, or, signed in, that books you finish on Hardcover appear there.

### Example profile

**View example profile** shows a read-only copy of the owner's recent reading, so someone without a Hardcover account can see what the app makes of a library. It opens on **Library**, and every tab shows a bar with "Example profile" and **Connect Hardcover**, which opens the Hardcover sheet; the bar is dark above Library and Desk, to match their backdrop. The **Spines** tab is hidden.

- **Data.** The app bundles `ExampleProfile/`, the folder shape that `LocalSpineStore` reads, and `AppModel.showExample(from:)` decodes it off the main actor. `script/export-example-profile.py` makes it from the signed-in Mac app's launch cache (`~/Library/Containers/gay.ian.Bookworms.Mac/Data/Library/Caches/Bookworms/library-snapshot.json`). It keeps the books on the **Read** shelf finished on or after January 1, 2024, and only their finish dates since then, with the series, designs, series looks, chosen covers, and series links they use; the choices become `cover-choices-v1.json` and `series-links-v1.json` records, as preview mode keeps them, and the series looks follow the links. It drops reviews and Hardcover's community ratings, so the details show no **Readers' rating**, and moves every record to the account ID `example`, which no Hardcover account matches, with record names and work-item hashes recomputed. It calls no API and reads no token or Keychain item. Covers and fonts download on first use, as for any library.
- **Private data.** The folder is the owner's real reading history, shipped with his consent. Git ignores everything in it except a placeholder (`.gitkeep`) that keeps the project's folder reference valid, so never commit it, print its contents, or use it in tests. A build without the data hides **View example profile**; a Release build warns without it, and an archive fails (see [docs/RELEASING.md](docs/RELEASING.md)).
- **Read-only.** The example lives in memory. `AppModel` never reloads, caches, or saves it: `reload()`, the launch-cache write, both `apply` methods, recolors, font picks, cover choices, and series choices return early, and `GenerationController` starts no run. Book details hide **Pin to Top Five**, the **Spine design** card, and the new-cover redesign card, and offer neither the spine tap that scrolls to that card nor VoiceOver's **Show spine design** action; **Series** hides its check buttons; context menus and the Mac's **Library** menu hide **Change Cover…** and **Redesign Spine**; and the Mac dims **Generate Now**, **Sync Library**, **Go > Spines**, and **Open in New Window**, shows no Dock menu, and opens no book windows. Library's sync status is hidden.
- **Share.** The Share tab offers only **My reading**, without the templates the example can't fill (**Review**, **Currently Reading**, **Dropped**, and **Hot Takes**), and ignores Top Five's pins. Its template and time frame choices aren't saved, and leaving the example restores the saved ones. Images show in the preview but never leave the app: the Share toolbar's **Share**, **Video**, and **Quick Look** are hidden, the Mac's **Save Image…**, **Copy Image**, and **Quick Look** menu items are dimmed, drag is off, and the Desk scene's and Dominoes' **Share** are hidden.
- **Time.** Time filters treat the snapshot's sync date as today (`AppModel.now`), so Library's **Last 12 Months** never empties as the snapshot ages. The Desk's **Last 12 Months** counts back from the same date, and gives way to the newest year with books when it has none (`TimeFrame.pileFrame(setting:in:now:)`). The Desk's and Library's **Sort by** and time choices, including the Mac's **View** menu, stay in memory (`AppModel.exampleChoices`) and start from the saved ones, which leaving the example restores.
- **Wording.** First-person labels change where they would describe the owner's reading as the viewer's: Library's **Your rating** order reads **Rating**, and **Series** says "Read 3 of 5." The share images keep their own titles, such as "My top 5 of 2025", since they never leave the app.
- **Ending it.** **Leave example profile** returns to **No library yet** and schedules the background generation run again (`AppModel.leaveExample()`), which the example skipped. Any change to the Hardcover account ends it before the account's library loads, and a relaunch starts without it. Tips that the example uses up, such as the Desk's, stay used up.

### Spine design model

Each run designs with the best model tier this device can use when the run starts, or with your model first when you choose it instead of Private Cloud Compute (see item 2), and keeps that tier for the whole run unless your provider rejects its settings, such as its key, model name, or account, which moves the rest of the run, and later runs until you change the model settings or start **Generate now** or **Redesign spine**, to the next tier, as described below (`ModelTier`, `BookAnalyzer.status(for:)`). The code calls a provider you add with your API key the backup model.

1. **Private Cloud Compute**, in builds with its managed entitlement, whenever the system reports it available and able to read images, unless your model designs instead of it (see item 2). Over its daily limit, the run waits for the reset instead of switching models. Builds compiled with `NO_PCC`, such as the ones `script/install_on_device.sh --no-pcc` and `script/run_mac.sh --no-pcc` make, leave this tier out, and their text never names it as the device's model; **Saved designs** still credits Private Cloud Compute with the designs it made (see [Designs from several devices](#designs-from-several-devices)).
2. **Your model with an API key**, when Private Cloud Compute isn't available, such as on a device that doesn't support it, with Apple Intelligence off, or in a `NO_PCC` build. **Choose model** in the Spines tab on the iPhone, which opens **Design model**, or **Settings > Design Model** on the Mac, which the Spines tab's **Choose model…** opens, sets **Model** to Anthropic, OpenAI, Google Gemini, or any OpenAI-compatible endpoint with its base URL. In builds with Private Cloud Compute, **Use this model instead of Private Cloud Compute**, off by default, puts this tier first: your model designs whenever it can, and Private Cloud Compute designs only while your model can't, such as without its key or after a rejection (`SpineDesigner.overridesPrivateCloudCompute`). The default, **Apple (default)**, which designs with Private Cloud Compute when it is available and otherwise with the on-device model (**Apple on-device (default)** in `NO_PCC` builds), skips this tier, and so does a provider without the API key, model name, or base URL it needs on this device, or one that rejected these settings, until you change them or start **Generate now** or **Redesign spine** (see below); the Spines tab then says what it is missing or shows the rejection.
3. **Apple's on-device model** (`SystemLanguageModel.default`), which needs Apple Intelligence and has no daily limit. It reads the title crop when it accepts images; otherwise each design comes from the title measurements alone, as when a guardrail rejects the crop.
4. **Local defaults**, which the Spines tab calls **Default styling**, with no model, when none of the others is available, such as on a device without Apple Intelligence and without a provider. The design still comes from the cover's colors and the title measurements, uses the same Google Fonts families and glyph checks, and passes the same validation. When the chosen family can't download, the design uses the system typeface of that family's style group with no font family, and the same contrast check, so a series copy of it needs no download either. That design records `font:system` in `provenance` and ranks below the tier's other designs. The app checks the book again after a day, and the first run that can download the font, at any tier, replaces the design and the series look made from it, unless you chose their colors or edited the look.

The Spines tab's **Design model on this iPhone** section, **Design model on this Mac** on the Mac, names the tier this device designs with in its **Model** row, such as **Private Cloud Compute**, **Google Gemini with your API key**, **OpenAI-compatible endpoint**, which may need no key, **Apple on-device model**, or **Default styling**, and says when that tier is unavailable. Under Private Cloud Compute, a **Daily limit** row says whether the limit is not reached, almost reached, or reached, with its reset time near or at the limit. On the iPhone, **Choose model** shows the current choice, **Apple** (**Apple on-device** in `NO_PCC` builds) or the provider's name, or what a provider you chose is missing, such as its API key or model name, or the provider's rejection; on the Mac, a **Chosen model** row under **Choose model…** shows what is missing or the rejection. While the last run's message starts with that rejection, **Choose model** shows the provider's name instead and the Mac shows no **Chosen model** row, so the rejection shows once. While Apple's on-device model or default styling designs, the section's footer suggests what would design better spines: "For better spines, turn on Apple Intelligence in the Settings app, or choose Anthropic, OpenAI, or Google Gemini and add your API key." (**System Settings** on the Mac) while Apple Intelligence is off and **Model** in the settings is Apple's, and only the part that applies when one of these holds. Otherwise the section has no footer, because **Choose model** says what a chosen provider is missing. The info button beside the model's name, whose hover text and VoiceOver hint say "Show details about this model", says in at most three short lines what a new spine costs, as about one request per new spine because a series copy sends none: "Free within Apple's daily limit, at about one request per new spine." for Private Cloud Compute, "About one request per new spine." for your model, "Free, with no daily limit." for the on-device model, and "No model is available." for default styling.
