- **Paper props.** `Bookworms/Views/Stationery.swift` holds the card stock that the library card, the bookplate, and, with catalog cards, a book's date-due card and review page are printed on (`CardStockSheet`), with inks that keep 7:1 on it (`StationeryTests`), a bookmark `Ribbon`, `SoftShadow`, a baked image (`SoftShadow`) stretched under a flat object so no blur runs, and the rubber stamps (`StampButtonStyle`), whose uneven ink a canvas draws once (`StampInk`). A `RestampButton` restamps its label when a tap changes it. One animated clock (`StampStage.clock`, read through `RestampClock` on the `RestampTimeline`) drives every frame of a restamp: a shader (`Restamp.metal`, `RestampEffect`) that soaks in the new label once the stamp clears it; `StampFall`, a still of the stamp baked from a 3D model with its shadows, which comes down on the tag; and the frame the stamp touches the paper, where the new label lands and the thunk and the haptic tap play. Each window's root view (`RootView`, and on the Mac `BookWindow`) applies `stampStage()`, which draws `StampFall` in an overlay above the whole window, without hit testing, at the button's frame in the window, which the button reports at a tap and as it moves during a restamp, so no card or scroll view clips the stamp, and it passes over the navigation and tab bars and under the iPhone's status bar. If the button leaves the screen during a restamp, the stamp stops. A `RestampButton` outside a `stampStage()` changes its label at once. The overlay always holds the stamp, too faint to see at rest, over the last label it restamped or, before any restamp, at the window's top leading corner, so a tap makes no view, a scroll at rest doesn't redraw it, and the stamp's images are ready. The baked still keeps the restamp cheap and steady: no 3D scene is made at a tap, and moving the still is moving the stamp, since the still is drawn through a long lens, as the flat page is. With uneven ink, each label has a printed copy of its own, so no copy draws another label during a restamp: at rest only the shown label's copy prints, a press prints the other one out of sight so it is ready to land, and the copy under a press keeps its pressed look until the stamp covers it. The shader runs only while the new label soaks in, and once a launch, unless Reduce Motion is on, the text is at an accessibility size, or the ink is solid, on a speck out of sight a second after the first such button appears (`RestampEffect.Primer`), since the first draw through the shader sets up its pipeline and holds up drawing for a moment. The button also plays `StampThunk`, a knock synthesized as the Desk's are (`ImpactSound.knock`) through its own `ImpactAudio`, which leaves the audio session active so the Desk's sounds keep playing. A `TapedTag`'s tape lies under its content, so the stamp passes over it. The props keep their colors in both appearances, as real cards would. Give a prop in a form row about 24 points of row inset, or the row clips its shadow.
- **Room scenes.** `Bookworms/Views/RoomScenes.swift` draws the empty rooms: `ShortShelf`, a short bookcase from Shelf's own pieces (`Bookcase`, `BookcaseWood`), brass `Bookend`s, plain-cloth spines, a blotter, and the baked desk still (`DeskStill`).
- **Template tiles.** `script/template-art.py` draws each share template's tile as an SVG image set, `template.<ShareTemplate raw value>`: a small dark card, as every share image is dark, in the Portrait shape, with the template's layout in cream and gold, except that Checkout Card's tile keeps the card stock and inks of its image, and Dropped's books are gray, as its image letters them. Its books keep book proportions and stand upright, fan like a hand of cards as Top Five's do, or lie flat. Change the script and run it again rather than editing the SVGs. Tiles are static images, never live renders: a share image renders at full size on the main actor, and the Desk template needs RealityKit.
- **Type.** `bookHeading()` sets headings, such as section headers and empty-state titles, in New York, the serif of book titles and share images. `Font.catalogLabel` sets card captions and stat labels in footnote-size semibold small caps, like a library catalog card. A book's paper details print their field names in `Font.detailsFieldLabel`, the same style, which on the Mac starts from body size, because the Mac's footnote is 10 points. Controls and rows stay in SF.
- **Symbols.** The asset catalog's custom symbols are named for where they're used: `tab.share` and `tab.spines`, with `.fill` variants that the tab bar shows, `menubar.bookworm`, and `account.bookworms`, the icon's two bookworms behind an open book, which the bookplate draws until it has the reader's Hardcover photo. `script/custom-symbols.py` draws them as SF Symbols variable templates at three weights, from which the system interpolates every weight and scale; change the script and run it again, or refine an SVG in the SF Symbols app. The menu bar symbol has no variable-value layer for generation progress.
- **Textures.** `script/room-textures.swift` bakes `WallGrain` from `TableWood`, as lightness alone around the wall's middle color so it never tints or lightens the wall, the `PaperDither` tile, the `SoftShadow` image, and `DeskStill`, the empty desk under the lamp, as HEIF with edges that fade to transparent. `script/stamp-still.py` bakes, in Blender, the desk stamp that restamps the Top Five tag (`StampStill`) and its shadow touching and lifted off the paper (`StampShadowNear`, `StampShadowFar`) from the model's USDZ, which is not in the repository (see [Credits](#credits)), roughening its glossy varnish, rubber, and brass, and prints the rubber face's corners and the shadows' frames that `StampFall` places them by. The three images are about 56 KB.
- **Time frames.** `TimeFrame.title` and `LibraryTime.title` name every time frame in Library, Desk, and Share: "All time" for every book, a year such as "2025", and a month such as "August 2025". Read the names from them rather than writing them out. Menu items use `menuTitle`, the same names in title case, such as **All Time** and **Last 12 Months**, as the HIG asks; text and controls in a window, such as Library's status and Share's **Time frame**, use `title`.
- **Mac menu commands.** Each area keeps its menu commands in a `Commands` type of its own file and registers it with one line in `BookwormsApp`'s `commands`. The app's own **Go** menu stays in `BookwormsCommands`; other areas add to system menus with `CommandGroup`. Views publish what the commands act on with `focusedSceneValue(_:_:in:)`, through the keys that `Bookworms/App/FocusedActions.swift` declares: `goBack`, `detailsBook`, `centeredBook`, `copyShareImage`, `saveShareImage`, `previewShareImage`, `showAccount`, and `libraryActions`. It names the view's tab and publishes only while that tab is selected, because the tab view keeps hidden tabs' views, such as details waiting for your return from **Share**. Library's and Desk's details, and pushed details, publish `goBack`; Library publishes `libraryActions` and, once scrolling settles, `centeredBook`, which is the first book until another is centered; the root view publishes `showAccount`; and the Share tab publishes the actions that `ShareCommands` runs, each of which reads the image when it runs. `LibraryCommands` reads Library's for the **File**, **View**, and **Library** menus. A view that publishes an action makes it once and keeps it in its state, so its body can run again, and the Share tab's image can change, without changing the published value and rebuilding the menus.
- **Tips.** `BookwormsApp` configures TipKit once at launch (`configureTips()`), so an area adds a `Tip` type and shows it with `popoverTip` or `TipView`. Debug builds accept `-BookwormsTips <show|hide|reset>` (see [Preview with a local library](#preview-with-a-local-library)).

## How the 3D books work

`Book3DView` draws each visible face of a book (cover, spine, page edges) as an ordinary SwiftUI view and maps it onto the screen with the perspective transform a pinhole camera gives that face. The faces share one camera, so their edges meet, and the result stays pure SwiftUI, so `ImageRenderer` can export it in share images, which it cannot do for RealityKit views. The details, the flight off the shelf, and share images draw a bound book (`BoundBook`): boards as thick as `BookShape` makes them, which on a hardcover overhang the pages by 3.5 mm around a striped headband; a hardcover's French groove beside the flat spine; rounded outer edges that catch the light; a soft shadow where the leaves meet the boards; and a blend across the fold between the cover and the spine: the colors along the cover's spine-side edge fade a tenth of the way across the spine, short of its bands and lettering, and the cover and the spine's cloth fade 3 mm onto the front board. `CoverStore` samples those edge colors once, as it decodes each cover. **Stack** draws plain boxes, which cost about half as much to draw, except for the book being pulled out. In 3D, as the stack scrolls, a visual effect corrects each box's faces for where the box lies from the stack's one camera, without updating the box's body; while the scroll view bounces past an end, it measures each box from where the box lies at that end (`ScrollEye.span`), as the board's top and the drawing order do, and a book changes its drawing order against its neighbors only as its top or bottom crosses the eye's level. Pile share images, from **Share desk** or the Share tab's Desk template, are the exception: they render RealityKit offscreen (see [Share the desk](#share-the-desk)).

The 3D views share their RealityKit pieces. `BookGeometry` builds each book's mesh from its `BookShape`, and `BookFactory` gives the book its textures, materials, physics box, and mass. `DeskStage` holds the desk, the lamp, the room's light, and the dust. `ImpactFeedback` turns collisions into taps and sounds, and can record each hit whose sound passes its throttle, whether or not **Sounds** is on: its time, and the sound's variant, volume, and pan, and the [videos](#video-format) mix the same `ImpactSound` samples from these records. `BookLifter` lifts a book out of the Desk scene or Dominoes into its details and returns it, thrown back into the pile or set down where it lay among the dominoes, with `BookFlight` giving its path, and `LiftedDetails` shows those details over the scene. `DeskScenes` holds the Desk scene and the dominoes, which share one desk and one set of book parts, and hands the books from one to the other, and `DeskSceneView` hosts their one `ARView` while Desk is on screen, except while a resting desk shows a still in its place (see [Desk](#desk)). `DominoSetup` gives the books' moves as they set themselves up as dominoes, `DominoFinale` the dominoes' gathering into the spread, and `DominoMovieTiming` the frames and hits that a recording of a run keeps.

## Run locally

1. Install [XcodeGen](https://github.com/yonaskolb/XcodeGen) and run `xcodegen generate --spec project.yml` to create `Bookworms.xcodeproj`. Source folders are synced, so new files need no regeneration.
2. Set up signing for `gay.ian.Bookworms.iOS` with the `iCloud.gay.ian.Bookworms` container, push notifications, and the managed Private Cloud Compute capability (`com.apple.developer.private-cloud-compute`), which the team holds; turn it on for the App ID in Certificates, Identifiers & Profiles, or a device build's signing fails (see [docs/RELEASING.md](docs/RELEASING.md)).
3. Run `script/build_and_run.sh` to build, install, and launch on a Simulator.

To include the [example profile](#example-profile), open the signed-in Mac app so its launch cache is current, then run `python3 -I script/export-example-profile.py --out ExampleProfile` before building. The script prints only counts: books kept, series, designs, looks, chosen covers, and series links. Builds without the folder's data hide **View example profile**, and Release builds, such as `script/run_mac.sh` and `script/install_on_device.sh`, warn; only an archive fails.

To test the Mac app with your iCloud library, run `script/run_mac.sh`. It builds an optimized app with Private Cloud Compute, signs it with the team's Apple Development certificate, `Bookworms/Bookworms-macOS.entitlements`, and the newest cached Xcode-managed "Mac Team Provisioning Profile: gay.ian.Bookworms.Mac", installs it as `~/Applications/Bookworms.app`, and opens it; Xcode's account is not needed while that profile is current. The profile must grant the shared iCloud container, push, and the managed Private Cloud Compute capability. Before it builds, the script checks the profile for `com.apple.developer.private-cloud-compute`, and without it stops with what to do: turn on Private Cloud Compute for the App ID `gay.ian.Bookworms.Mac` in Certificates, Identifiers & Profiles, then let Xcode refresh the profile with the `xcodebuild -allowProvisioningUpdates` command it prints, which needs the team's account in **Xcode > Settings > Accounts**. `script/run_mac.sh --no-pcc` builds the variant without Private Cloud Compute instead, with `config/NoPCC-macOS.entitlements` and `NO_PCC`, as `script/install_on_device.sh --no-pcc` does for the iPhone: the provider you choose in Settings designs spines, with your API key or a keyless OpenAI-compatible endpoint, else Apple's on-device model, else local defaults (see [Spine design model](#spine-design-model)). The Mac build uses the bundle ID `gay.ian.Bookworms.Mac`, so it replaces an installed Bookworms Spines menu bar companion. A Mac build with any other bundle ID, such as an Xcode Debug build's `gay.ian.Bookworms.iOS` or a scratch copy's, marks itself as a test copy (`TestCopy`): its Dock icon turns grayscale, and each window's title bar says **Test copy**. A Release build with `gay.ian.Bookworms.iOS`, such as an archive for TestFlight or the Mac App Store, doesn't mark itself. `script/run_mac.sh --preview`, which combines with `--no-pcc`, copies the private preview library into the app's container and reads it instead of iCloud.

The iPhone and the Mac share `Bookworms/AppIcon.icon`, an Icon Composer icon over a cream gradient: two worms read an open book whose cover runs past the bottom and both sides. It has two groups, so the system shadows the book onto the worms. The front group holds the book (`book.png`), on a glass layer. The back group holds the pupils and smiles (`face.png`), then the eye whites (`eyes.png`), then the worms (`worms.png`), on a glass layer. The eye whites are filled white in the dark, clear, and tinted appearances so the eyes stay white on a dark icon; the light icon keeps their cream. The layers are flat color with one soft shade tone and no highlights, because the system adds the glass. A Blender script in the Apple TV repository, [`design/icon/bookworms.py`](https://github.com/ianhyzy/bookworms-tvos/blob/main/design/icon/bookworms.py), models the icon and renders the four layers: run `blender -b -P design/icon/bookworms.py -- --square <this repository>/Bookworms/AppIcon.icon/Assets` there. To preview every appearance, export the icon with Icon Composer's `ictool` (`Xcode.app/Contents/Applications/Icon Composer.app/Contents/Executables/ictool`).

To test on an iPhone, run `script/install_on_device.sh`. It signs an optimized build with `Bookworms/Bookworms.entitlements`, which includes Private Cloud Compute, lets Xcode's automatic signing update the profile, registers the iPhone with the team if needed, and installs it. Signing needs the team's account in **Xcode > Settings > Accounts** and Private Cloud Compute turned on for the App ID `gay.ian.Bookworms.iOS`; when the build fails on either, the script says what to do. `script/install_on_device.sh --no-pcc` instead signs with `config/NoPCC.entitlements` and compiles with `NO_PCC`, so the app never creates the Private Cloud Compute model. That build skips the Private Cloud Compute tier and never names it: the provider you choose designs, with your API key or a keyless OpenAI-compatible endpoint, else Apple's on-device model, else local defaults (see [Spine design model](#spine-design-model)). A build with Private Cloud Compute later redesigns, within its daily limit, only the spines that Apple's on-device model or default styling made, except spines whose colors the reader chose; it keeps the provider's. The Share, Library, and Desk tabs work with the iCloud or Hardcover library. A development-signed build reads the CloudKit Development environment, the same one development builds of the Apple TV app use.

The app shows the library it loaded last while it reloads. It reloads when it becomes active, at most every five minutes after a load succeeds. After a failed load, it tries again the next time it becomes active or a generation run ends. It also reloads after a generation run that saved designs for books it had not loaded. Without a Hardcover sign-in, signing out of iCloud, switching to an account without a library, turning iCloud storage off, iCloud holding several libraries with none chosen, or choosing another library clears the shown and cached library. Signing out of Hardcover, or signing in to another Hardcover account, also clears it before the reload, which then shows the iCloud archive's library or the new account's. Every reload, including those after a sign-in or sign-out, first runs the iCloud account check (see [One Hardcover account in iCloud](#one-hardcover-account-in-icloud)).

### Preview with a local library

The Simulator usually has no iCloud library. `script/load-preview-data.py` converts a private Apple TV library export and its fonts from the workspace `.local` folder into the iCloud JSON shapes and copies them into the installed app, or with `--caches DIR` into that `Caches/Bookworms` folder, such as a scratch Mac home's. Then run `script/build_and_run.sh --preview`, which launches with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview`. Generation then reads and writes that folder instead of iCloud. Model requests are real: Private Cloud Compute's count against the signed-in Apple Account's daily limit, and a provider's are billed to your API account.

The preview data contains real reading history. It stays in ignored `.local/` folders and the Simulator; publish screenshots made from it only when the owner chooses his library for App Store screenshots (see the release workflow's [Screenshots](docs/RELEASING.md#screenshots)).

Preview mode keeps cover choices in `cover-choices-v1.json`, series choices in `series-links-v1.json`, and series looks in `series-looks-v1.json` in the preview folder. Loading the preview data again keeps those files. Deleting the choices restores the default covers and series; deleting the looks lets the next reload create them again from the saved designs.

Debug builds accept more launch arguments for checks that cannot tap the Simulator, such as `-BookwormsGenerateOnLaunch YES`, `-BookwormsTab <share|library|desk|spines>` (opens that tab), `-BookwormsPointerLean <-1...1>` (turns every Library book as the pointer would on the Mac: 1 at its trailing end, -1 at its leading end; start a negative value with a space, such as `" -1"`), `-BookwormsSpinesScroll YES` (scrolls the Spines tab to **Saved designs**), `-BookwormsShelfMode <stack|shelf|pile>` (`stack` and `shelf` open Library with that view, and `pile` opens Desk; the launch keeps Library's view, so check **View as** in a launch without it), `-BookwormsLibraryTime <last12Months|thisYear|last5Years|allTime|year:2025>` (sets Library's time for that launch), `-BookwormsStackStyle <2d|3d>` (draws **Stack** flat or in 3D; the launch keeps that choice, so check **3D Books** in a launch without it), `-BookwormsShelfScrollTo <index>` (centers the book at that index, counting from 0, in **Stack** and **Shelf**), `-BookwormsShelfGlideTo <index>` (centers the book at that index three seconds after the library loads, gliding there when it lies within half the stack's height of the center, so a recording shows the books scroll), `-BookwormsFindBook <text>` (searches for that text when Library appears, in the Mac's search field or the iPhone's **Find a book** sheet), `-BookwormsShelfSweep <count>` (moves the center to the next book that many times, every 0.15 seconds, from three seconds after the library loads, so a CPU or frame-time recording sees **Stack** or **Shelf** scroll without a touch), `-BookwormsOpenBook <book ID>` (in **Stack** and **Shelf**, centers that book and pulls it out to its details two seconds later, and with `-BookwormsPullHold <0...1>`, stops that flight at that progress, without the details, so a screenshot shows one moment of it; in the Desk scene and Dominoes, the scene lifts that book four seconds after its drop finishes or its books stand, and `buried` in place of an ID lifts the book with the most books over it; the other 3D views ignore it), and `-BookwormsShareTemplate <yearInBooks|topFive|hotTakes|pile|justFinished|review|currentlyReading|tasteMatch|bookSwap|checkoutCard|dropped>` with `-BookwormsShareCard <n>` (Checkout Card's card, counting from 1) and `-BookwormsShareFrame <year:2025|month:2025-08|lifetime>` and `-BookwormsShareCover <threeD|flat>`, which writes each render, with the credit as sharing adds it, to `Library/Caches/Bookworms/share-debug.png`. `-BookwormsShareTemplate` also saves its template and mode as the studio's choices, with the size that `-BookwormsShareAspect` names, else Story, and the time frame that `-BookwormsShareFrame` names, else the newest year, so a later launch without these arguments opens on them. `-BookwormsShareMode <mine|friends>` chooses **My reading** or **With friends** (Taste Match and Book Swap choose **With friends** on their own), `-BookwormsShareFriends YES` replaces Hardcover with four fictional readers whose ratings derive from the shown library, also in preview mode (see [Check the friends' templates](#check-the-friends-templates)), `-BookwormsShareReader <username>` compares with that reader, and `-BookwormsShareFullSize YES` shows each render full size, as holding the preview does, `-BookwormsShareAvatar <URL>` gives the fictional owner of `-BookwormsShareFriends YES` that profile photo, which loads as covers do, so an image saved in `Library/Caches/Bookworms/covers` under the SHA-256 of the URL draws without a download, and `-TopFivePins <IDs>`, such as `-TopFivePins 12,34`, pins those books for that launch; pinning in the app doesn't show during such a launch, since the argument overrides the saved pins. The preview library has no reviews or current reads; `-BookwormsShareSamples <n>` adds fictional ones to Debug builds, in the Share, Library, and Desk tabs, where the pile's **All Time** shows the current reads' ribbons. For the pile, `-BookwormsPileZoom <1...2.5>` and `-BookwormsPileYaw <radians>` set the camera, and once a drop finishes, `-BookwormsPileFocus <book ID>` zooms all the way in on that book three seconds later, `-BookwormsPileTilt <x,y>` applies a tilt, `-BookwormsPileShake <x,y,z>` shakes the phone four seconds later with that user acceleration, in g in the device's axes (x toward the screen's right, y toward its top, z out of the glass), and `-BookwormsPileThrow YES` picks up the top book four seconds later, carries it up the screen and to the right, and throws it on. `-BookwormsPileWakeTap <seconds>` taps the middle of the screen that long after each pause of the desk, as a quick tap does, and `-BookwormsReducedEffects YES` saves power as Low Power Mode does. `-BookwormsPileGravity <x,y,z>` stands in for the device's gravity in the same axes while the pile follows motion, and shows **Tilt** in the menu while it is offered, so `-BookwormsPileTiltMode box -BookwormsPileGravity 0,0,1` holds the phone face down. Start a vector that begins with a minus sign with a space, such as `-BookwormsPileGravity " -1,0,0"`, because a launch argument that starts with a minus sign names another key. `-BookwormsPileTiltMode <off|table|box>` sets **Tilt**, which counts as **Off** while it is hidden, and `-BookwormsPileByPeriod YES` sets the drop by period. `-BookwormsPileBack <seconds>` presses **Back** that many seconds after the details of a book lifted from the Desk scene or Dominoes or pulled off the shelf appear. `-BookwormsLiftLog YES` logs how the books on a book lifted from the Desk scene or Dominoes move (see [Check a book's flight to its details](#check-a-books-flight-to-its-details)). `-BookwormsSetUpDominoes YES` chooses **Set up dominoes** two seconds after the desk's first drop finishes, `-BookwormsDominoesFromAbove YES` makes that choice leave the desk's books behind, so the chain stands up from above, as another time frame's chain does, and `-BookwormsSetupLog YES` logs the setup (see [Check the dominoes' setup](#check-the-dominoes-setup)). `-BookwormsPileFrame <last12Months|year:2025|month:2025-8>` sets the time frame of the 3D views, and `-BookwormsPileGather <seconds>` gathers the books that long after a drop finishes, as a double tap on the bare desk does. `-BookwormsPileShare YES` opens **Share desk** and writes each image, with its credit, to `Library/Caches/Bookworms/pile-share-debug.png`, `-BookwormsPileShareAspect <story|portrait|square>` picks its size, and `-BookwormsPileShareSeed <n>` its first seed. With it, `-BookwormsPileVideo YES` also opens the video sheet and copies each finished video to `Library/Caches/Bookworms/pile-video-debug.mp4`, with its Live Photo copy at `pile-video-debug.mov` and its still at `pile-video-debug.jpg`, and `-BookwormsPileShareGlare YES` turns every book of a share image so its cover reflects the lamp most brightly (see [Check the covers' finish under the lamp](#check-the-covers-finish-under-the-lamp)). `-BookwormsDominoVideo YES` copies each recording of a dominoes run to `domino-video-debug.mp4` and `domino-video-debug.png`, and `-BookwormsImpactLog YES` logs every hit of a 3D scene to `impact-log.csv`. `-BookwormsPileRedrops <n>` drops the pile again until drop n, or, with **Share desk** open, renders the next seed until seed n. `-BookwormsPileCoverLog YES` logs how the books land, and for share images and videos how much of each cover shows (see [Check how books land](#check-how-books-land) and [Check a share pile's covers](#check-a-share-piles-covers)). `-BookwormsSeriesSheet YES` opens **Series** from the **Series** row or card of a book's details, `-BookwormsDetailScroll <points|end>` scrolls the details that far down, or to the end, a second after they appear, `-BookwormsTapBook <x,y>` taps the details' book at that point, in points from the top leading corner of its 460-point stage, three seconds after it appears, `-BookwormsHandFallback YES` keeps Noteworthy in place of Caveat on the catalog cards, `-BookwormsStampPressed YES` prints every stamped button as pressed, `-BookwormsStampHovered YES` prints every enabled one as under the Mac's pointer, `-BookwormsRestampDemo <seconds>` taps every enabled restamping stamp, which today is only **Pin to Top Five**, that many seconds after it appears and three more times 2 seconds apart; 2 seconds later, twice 0.15 seconds apart, as the stamp comes down; and 2 seconds after that, twice 0.8 seconds apart, as the new label soaks in, leaving the pin as it was, and `-BookwormsDesigningSpines YES` shows a run's progress in place of every redesign button, without a run. `-BookwormsOfferedHardcover YES` skips the Hardcover sheet that the first launch without `-BookwormsPreviewFolder` opens when this device is not signed in, and `NO` offers it again; a rejected login still opens it. With `-BookwormsShowSpines YES`, `-BookwormsShowAccount YES` opens the gear's **Settings** on the iPhone, where `-BookwormsSettingsScroll YES` scrolls to **Credits**, the form's end, a second later, and the Hardcover sheet on the Mac, where `-BookwormsShowSettings YES` opens the Settings window, on the pane that `-BookwormsSettingsPane <general|backupModel|hardcover>` names (`backupModel` is **Design Model**), and `-BookwormsStubSignIn YES` answers sign-in with the fictional code `WORM-2611` that is never approved, without contacting Hardcover. `-BookwormsLibraryStatus <signedOut|downloading|failed|reconnect>` makes the empty Share, Library, and Desk tabs show that state with a fictional error, or for `downloading` fictional progress. With `-BookwormsPreviewFolder`, `-BookwormsAccountPrompt <other|choose|mergeFailed|deletion>` shows one iCloud account prompt at launch with fictional accounts: `other` asks to merge @maple_reader's data, `choose` asks which of @maple_reader's and @birch_pages's libraries to show, `mergeFailed` shows **Couldn't merge**, and `deletion` shows how to delete Bookworms data from iCloud after **Keep iCloud Off**; without a preview folder, the launch's iCloud account check can replace the `other` and `choose` prompts. `-BookwormsShareTabs "desk:0 share:8"` selects each tab, named as `-BookwormsTab` names it, once its seconds pass after the previous entry. The schedule starts when the Share tab first appears, so also pass `-BookwormsShareTemplate <name>` to open that tab at launch. With `-BookwormsOpenBook <book ID>`, `-BookwormsSpineColors "RRGGBB,RRGGBB,RRGGBB"` picks those background, text, and accent colors on that book's **Spine design** card once per launch, as its color pickers would, and an empty entry, as in `"RRGGBB,,"`, keeps that color, `-BookwormsFontPicker YES` opens its font picker, or with `large` at full height, on the iPhone `-BookwormsColorPicker <background|text|accent>` opens that color's picker, `-BookwormsFontSearch <text>` opens the picker's **Other** with that search, and `-BookwormsSpineFont <family>` chooses that family in the picker, which saves it, and `-BookwormsCoverPicker YES` opens **Change cover**; `-BookwormsStubCovers YES` answers it with six fictional covers drawn on the device, without Hardcover or an image host, and `-BookwormsChooseCover <index>` chooses one of them; `-BookwormsStubCovers expired` answers as Hardcover does for a revoked login. `-BookwormsTips <show|hide|reset>` shows every tip whatever its rules, hides them all for screenshots, or forgets which tips were shown. `-BookwormsExampleProfile YES` opens the [example profile](#example-profile) at launch, in place of the launch's load, on a device without a library or Hardcover sign-in. Search the source for `"Bookworms` to find them all.

`-BookwormsPileFrame lifetime` also chooses **All Time**. A `-BookwormsPileFrame` argument fixes the time frame for that launch: the time frame button still saves a choice, but the 3D views use it only after you relaunch without the argument.

### Check a pile image beside the live pile

A script cannot tap tabs, so `-BookwormsShareTabs` switches them. To check that the Share tab's Desk template renders while the Desk tab holds a pile, including when the reader leaves mid-render:

1. Load the preview library, then launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsShelfMode pile -BookwormsShareTemplate pile -BookwormsShareFrame year:2025 -BookwormsShareTabs "desk:0 share:8 desk:0.8 share:4 desk:6"`.
2. Desk drops its pile, the Share tab starts a pile image, Desk returns 0.8 seconds into the render, and the Share tab renders again. Check that the app does not crash, that `Library/Caches/Bookworms/share-debug.png` holds the finished image, and that Desk drops its pile again at the end.

### Check a desk video

1. Load the preview library, then launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsShelfMode pile -BookwormsPileShare YES -BookwormsPileVideo YES`, adding `-BookwormsPileShareAspect portrait` or `square` for the other sizes.
2. Wait for the sheet to show the looping clip, then copy `Library/Caches/Bookworms/pile-video-debug.mp4`, `pile-video-debug.mov`, and `pile-video-debug.jpg` out of the app's data container (`xcrun simctl get_app_container booted gay.ian.Bookworms.iOS data`).
3. Check the MP4's format as [Check a video's format](#check-a-videos-format) describes: 180 frames at the chosen size, six seconds long. Run `ffprobe -show_streams -show_format pile-video-debug.mov` and check for the same video and sound, a timed metadata track, and the `com.apple.quicktime.content.identifier` tag.
4. Extract frames, such as `ffmpeg -i pile-video-debug.mp4 -vf fps=2 frame-%02d.png`, and check that the table starts empty, that each book falls into view from under the heading straight onto its place, that the books under a book land before it, and that the last 1.5 seconds hold still on the same arrangement as a share image of the same seed and size, with the heading and credit sharp in every frame. With `-BookwormsPileCoverLog YES`, the cover log's `video` lines measure the last frame as [Check a share pile's covers](#check-a-share-piles-covers) describes. Check a lifetime pile (`-BookwormsPileFrame lifetime`) and a month with one book too.
5. Decode the sound with `ffmpeg -i pile-video-debug.mp4 -map 0:a out.wav` and check that each knock starts with the frame where a book lands and comes from that side of the picture.

**Save as Live Photo** needs a person to allow adding to Photos; check that Photos shows the saved Live Photo and plays it.

### Check a Tall Stack image and its clip

1. Load the preview library, then launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsTab share -BookwormsShareTemplate tower -BookwormsShareBatch "tower,story,year:<YYYY> tower,portrait,year:<YYYY> tower,square,year:<YYYY> tower,story,month:<YYYY-MM> tower,story,lifetime"`, choosing a month of 16 books or fewer, so one image has no loupe, and a library of more than 150 books for **All Time**, so the cap applies. The Share tab writes each image to `Library/Caches/Bookworms/share-debug-<entry>.png`.
2. Check that every book lies level on the one below it, that the tape hangs plumb beside the tower without touching a book and its hook rests on the top book, that the worm sits coiled at the base, that the figure stands on the desk beside the tower, lit, with the line under the headline naming it, (add `-BookwormsTowerFigure <name>`, such as `windmill`, to stand any figure of `TowerFigure.ladder(readerMeters:)` beside the tower, and `-BookwormsReaderHeight <meters>` to set your height; `-BookwormsShareFriends YES -BookwormsShareAvatar <URL>` gives the figure a photo in preview mode, so one library checks every model), and that the loupe's titles read at phone size.
3. Launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsTab share -BookwormsShareTemplate tower -BookwormsShareFrame year:<YYYY> -BookwormsPileVideo YES`, adding `-BookwormsShareAspect portrait` or `square` for the other sizes. Once the first image draws, the video sheet opens, and the finished clip is copied to `pile-video-debug.mp4`, `.mov`, and `.jpg` as a desk video's are.
4. Extract frames, such as `ffmpeg -i pile-video-debug.mp4 -vf fps=10 frame-%02d.png`, and check that the falling books stay in view, that the figure may leave the frame during the fall and is back by the rewind's end, the tape is back in its case before books reach it, the loupe fades out and back, and the first and last frames match the still. Decode the sound as for a desk video and check that knocks start as books land and that the rewind is silent.

### Check how books land

1. Load the preview library, then launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsShelfMode pile -BookwormsPileCoverLog YES -BookwormsPileRedrops 5`, adding `-BookwormsPileFrame lifetime` for **All Time** or `-BookwormsPileByPeriod YES` for **Drop by month**.
2. Each live drop logs once its books come to rest. Read `Library/Caches/Bookworms/pile-cover-up.log`: each line counts the books that lie front cover up (cover within 45 degrees of straight up), face down, and on an edge or leaning; the book shapes, and so meshes, the pile uses; and the books' volume in liters and the height of the pile's top in scene units. Average several drops, since one drop of a few dozen books varies by several books. Expect about nine in ten front cover up, a little fewer with **Drop by month**. Share images and videos log the same line, with every book front cover up.

### Check a share pile's covers

1. Load the preview library, then launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsShareTemplate pile -BookwormsShareSeed <n> -BookwormsPileCoverLog YES -BookwormsShareBatch "pile,story,month:2025-07 pile,portrait,year:2025 pile,square,lifetime"`, choosing frames with few books, about 40, and the most, in each size. The Share tab writes each image to `Library/Caches/Bookworms/share-debug-<entry>.png`.
2. Read `Library/Caches/Bookworms/pile-cover-up.log`. Each image adds three lines: how the books lie (see [Check how books land](#check-how-books-land)); `covers shown`, the least and mean share of a front cover that the camera shows inside the pile rect, measured from the books as they lie (`PileSpread.visibility(of:camera:in:of:samples:)`), with the shares of the pile rect that covers and books fill and the steepest tilt; and `pixels`, the same share counted in rendered frames that draw the covers white on black, a few covers per frame. Each cover should show at least 80 percent by both measures, which agree within about a percent.
3. Look at the images for how the books fill the frame and lie on each other, and render a few seeds to check that **Shuffle** changes the arrangement. The images show the private preview library, so keep them local.

### Check the covers' finish under the lamp

A cover that tilts toward the camera reflects the lamp, and a live drop rarely repeats the same tilt, so this check turns every book of a share image to the angle where its cover reflects the lamp most brightly.

1. Load the preview library, then launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsShelfMode pile -BookwormsPileFrame year:2025 -BookwormsPileShare YES -BookwormsPileShareSeed 1 -BookwormsPileShareGlare YES`, and again without `-BookwormsPileShareGlare`. Each launch writes `Library/Caches/Bookworms/pile-share-debug.png`. With the argument, each book faces halfway between the lamp and the camera, lifted off the table, and books can pass through one another.
2. Compare covers with dark ink on a light ground, such as comics compendiums, in both images. Every cover should keep its dark ink dark and its art legible. To measure, take the 5th percentile of CIE L\* inside each cover as its black level: a cover's black level should rise by no more than about 15 between the flat and the turned image.

### Check sharing feedback and the credit

1. Load the preview library, then launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsShareTemplate justFinished`. Check that the preview shows no credit, and that `Library/Caches/Bookworms/share-debug.png` shows it centered at the bottom edge. A `-BookwormsShareBatch` run checks every template and size at once. Add `-BookwormsShareFriends YES` to give **My reading** images the fictional profile link "hardcover.app/@averyreads", and Review and Currently Reading the byline and chip of the fictional Avery Quill, with initials, or with `-BookwormsShareAvatar <URL>`, a fictional photo, and check that the preview shows it, that it sits above the credit on Portrait and Square and above the bottom 150 pixels on a Story, and that it doesn't touch the content above it. The preview library has no reviews or current reads, so check the byline and chip with fictional ones: add `-BookwormsShareSamples 0 -BookwormsShareBatch "review,story,lifetime currentlyReading,story,lifetime currentlyReading,square,lifetime"`, which writes each image to `Library/Caches/Bookworms/share-debug-<entry>.png`, with each `:` in the entry written as `_`. Check that Currently Reading's chip stacks on the Story and takes one line on Square. The preview library has no **Did Not Finish** books either: `-BookwormsShareDropped <n>` moves its first `n` unread books with covers to that shelf, dropped on the 15th of successive months this year, so `-BookwormsShareDropped 9 -BookwormsShareBatch "dropped,story,,threeD dropped,portrait,,flat"` checks both covers (with `3`) and spines.
2. Tap **Share**, then **Save Image**, and allow adding to Photos the first time. A "Saved to Photos" toast should slide in below the Dynamic Island and leave after about two seconds. **Copy** shows "Copied", and closing the sheet shows nothing.
3. Launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsShelfMode pile -BookwormsPileShare YES` and share from **Share desk**: the toast should show above the sheet, and the status bar should keep its style.
4. To see where a toast sits without sharing, such as on a Mac, launch a Debug build with `-BookwormsToast "Saved to Photos"`: four seconds after launch, that toast shows for 30 seconds. On a Mac, it should sit centered over the section's content, below the toolbar, not over the sidebar.

Simulator saves land in that Simulator's Photos library. A person checks VoiceOver's announcement, the Reduce Motion fade, and posting to Instagram on an iPhone.

### Check a book's flight to its details

1. Load the preview library, start recording the Simulator with `xcrun simctl io <device> recordVideo --codec h264 <file>`, and launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsShelfMode pile -BookwormsOpenBook buried -BookwormsPileBack 3`.
2. Step through the frames from the lift to the throw back, for example with `ffmpeg -vf fps=30`. The books over the lifted book rise with it or slide aside, then fall into its place and settle, and none stays in the air. After **Back**, the book crashes into the pile where it lay. Add `-BookwormsLiftLog YES` to log the lifted book's height and each book the lift woke, with its height and velocity, over the first 30 frames of the lift, to `Library/Caches/Bookworms/lift-log.csv`: the books on the lifted one rise with it.
3. Repeat with `-BookwormsSetUpDominoes YES -BookwormsDominoPush 1 -BookwormsPileFrame year:2025 -BookwormsDominoVideo YES`, which lifts the fallen book with the most books over it about three seconds into the run. The chain should hold still while the book is out, the books that fell onto it should make way without passing through it, as they or it move and as it comes back, the book should drop back exactly where it lay, and the run should go on from there, in the view and in `domino-video-debug.mp4`, without a jump. Add `-BookwormsLiftLog YES` and read `lift-exit.csv` to see how many books rested on it and which way it left. Repeat without `-BookwormsDominoPush` and with a book ID from the middle of the chain in place of `buried`, which lifts that book while the chain stands; it rises past its neighbors, then drops back into its place without starting the chain. Also tap books once the books lie gathered, which should drop back into their places in the spread; for a book that another lies across, which `lift-exit.csv` lists with a slide or a draw, step through the frames at 20 frames a second and check that no book passes through another on the way out or back. Touch and hold a standing book and drag, which should lift it rather than carry it and leave the view where it was after **Back**, and add `-BookwormsDominoCamera follow` to check that the camera gives way to the flight and returns to the camera mode's view in one move.
4. For **Stack**, launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsShelfMode stack -BookwormsStackStyle 3d -BookwormsOpenBook <book ID> -BookwormsPileBack 3`, with the ID of a thick book that lies turned, and step through the frames from the pull to the end of the return at 60 fps (`fps=60`). The book slides out of its gap, growing, and in 2D moving down the screen, before it rises, and the return slides it back in the same way. No part of it pops or fades where the flight takes over from the stack at the end of the slide, or hands back to it on the return, and the books above the gap shift up a few points as the slide starts, spreading outward from the gap, hold their places as the book slides back, then snap shut onto it, nearest first, within about a second of it landing. The books below the gap never move, and the bottom book never covers the board's front face. The book itself does not move in the first frame after the flight ends. Compare that frame with the last flight frame pixel by pixel, for a strongly turned book, in both styles. Repeat with a thin book, with `-BookwormsStackStyle 2d`, and with a book tapped near the top or the bottom of the visible stack, which glides to the center before it slides out. On the Mac, which has no Simulator recorder, record the window with ScreenCaptureKit or check stills: a Debug build launched with `-BookwormsPullHold 0.29` holds the book at the end of the stack's slide, just before the flight takes over, and `0.3` holds the flight's first frame. In a window wide enough for two stacks, check a long, strongly turned book at the outer end of each stack, in both styles, and the smallest window: no part of the book is cut off, and the two stills match. The recorder writes a frame only when the display changes, so a slide that does not move shows as a gap in the frame times.

### Check a book's details

1. Load the preview library, then launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsTab library -BookwormsShelfMode stack -BookwormsLibraryTime allTime -BookwormsOpenBook <book ID>`, and again with `-detailsNoteCards NO` for the glass cards.
2. Check a book with a title long enough to wrap, one with a long description, one without a description, an audiobook, a book without ratings, the default, xxxLarge, third, and largest accessibility text sizes, and both appearances. Tap **More** on a long description. Tap the book's spine, and its cover, with `-BookwormsTapBook <x,y>` where the Simulator can't tap. Add `-BookwormsHandFallback YES` to check the Noteworthy handwriting, `-BookwormsStampPressed YES` to check the pressed stamps, `-BookwormsStampHovered YES` to check the Mac's hover ink, and `-BookwormsDesigningSpines YES` to check the busy redesign stamp, also on the card that offers a redesign after **Change cover**. On the Mac, move the pointer over a stamp to check the pointing hand and the deeper ink. Check the stamped buttons with Increase Contrast on, and that no stamp's title breaks inside a word at the largest accessibility text size.
3. The preview library has no series, publication years, listening times, or reviews. To check those cards, copy the Simulator's `Library/Caches/Bookworms/preview` folder in the app's data container to a sibling folder, add `seriesID` with `seriesName` and `seriesPosition`, `publicationYear`, `audioSeconds`, or `review` to a few books in its `library-v1.json`, and launch with that folder's name in `-BookwormsPreviewFolder`. Delete the copy afterward; never edit the copy in `.local/`.
4. To check the **Spine design** card's **Series** and **Changes apply to** choices, also give a few designed books by one author a `seriesMemberships` list, such as `[{"seriesID":9001,"name":"Fictional Saga","position":1,"featured":true,"compilation":false,"size":3},{"seriesID":9002,"name":"Fictional Universe","featured":false,"compilation":false,"size":40}]`. The books should draw alike once the library loads, which also writes their look to `series-looks-v1.json`. Give one of them a name as long as "Discworld - Ankh-Morpork City Watch", and check at the default and the largest accessibility text sizes that **Series** wraps under its label without overlapping **Changes apply to**. Choose another series, **Not in a series**, and **This book only**, and check that the spine regroups at once and that `series-links-v1.json` holds the choice until you choose **Default** with **Whole series** again. Open **Change series cover** with `-BookwormsCoverPicker YES -BookwormsStubCovers YES` and check that the sheet is titled **Series cover** and names the book and series. Tap **Series** on the **Details** card, and the **Series** card with glass cards, tap the other series' check, and check that the list stays open and that the details and the **Spine design** card's **Series** menu follow. Tap each series and check that its books show in order with your books marked.
5. Tap **Make share image** and check that the window, including the tab bar or the Mac's sidebar and toolbar, cross-fades into the Share tab with the book, and that the book's details are still there when you return to the tab.
6. To check the font picker, launch with `-BookwormsFontPicker large` and check that each row sets the title in its family, that a family that lacks the title's glyphs is dimmed, and that the check marks the spine's family. Add `-BookwormsFontSearch <text>` to check **Other**, and `-BookwormsSpineFont <family>` to check that the card's spine, colors unchanged, takes the family and that `spine-designs-v1.json`, or `series-looks-v1.json` for a book that draws its series' look, records it with `reader-font`. The preview's own designs record no families they tried, so their pickers list only the spine's family; give a design in the copy a `choice` with `candidates`, as designs from a run record them.
7. To check the color resets, add `-BookwormsSpineColors "RRGGBB,,"` with a color unlike the design's background, and check that only **Background** has a reset, on catalog and glass cards and at an accessibility text size, and that tapping it restores the design's background and shows no reset. Add `-BookwormsColorPicker background` and check that the picker's sheet shows its current color, saved colors, and **+** on **Grid**, **Spectrum**, and **Sliders**, that the card's heading sits below the bar, and that the cover, the spine, lettered as the saved spine is, and the **Background** row with its reset stay above it. With a color that fails the contrast check, such as `"8E2B2B,8E2B2B,C9A227"`, the sheet should open at half height, with the contrast note above it. Tap the picker's eyedropper, drag it over the cover, and lift: the picker should come back, and the spine should show the cover's color. Tap the reset while the picker shows, then close the picker and tap a color at once: its picker should open once the first one leaves. Swipe it down. Then check that **Back**, **Make share image**, and, after you scroll up on a book with a series (step 4), **Series** each close the picker, and that **Series** opens. Repeat on an iPhone SE, where the sheet opens at half height.
8. To check the models that **Saved designs** on the Spines tab and **Model** on the **Spine design** card name, give designs in the copy's `spine-designs-v1.json` the generator `ios-pcc-v2` and `provenance` model tokens such as `model:google/gemini-3.8-flash`, `model:apple/on-device`, and `model:local`. The preview's own designs have no model token, so they read as **Default styling**.

### Check House of Leaves' blue

1. Copy the preview folder as [Check a book's details](#check-a-books-details) describes, and give House of Leaves (book ID `301814`) and another book with "house" in its title a fictional `review` that uses "house" in several forms, along with words such as "household". For Hot Takes, give House of Leaves a `rating` at least half a star above its `communityRating`.
2. Launch with that folder in `-BookwormsPreviewFolder`, `-BookwormsTab library -BookwormsLibraryTime allTime -BookwormsOpenBook 301814`, and each of `-BookwormsShelfMode stack` and `-BookwormsShelfMode shelf`, with and without `-detailsNoteCards NO`. Check the caption, the spine, the 3D book, the header, the description after **More**, and the review. Repeat with `-BookwormsTab desk -BookwormsOpenBook 301814`, with and without `-BookwormsSetUpDominoes YES`.
3. Add `-BookwormsSpineColors` with a light, a saturated, and a mid-blue cloth. This saves the colors to the copy.
4. Render the share templates with `-BookwormsShareTemplate review -BookwormsShareBook 301814 -BookwormsShareBatch "<entries>"`, with one entry for each template, size, and cover style, in the month the book was finished, such as `"review,story,month:<YYYY-MM> justFinished,square,month:<YYYY-MM>,flat topFive,story,month:<YYYY-MM> hotTakes,story,month:<YYYY-MM> yearInBooks,story,month:<YYYY-MM>"`. Remove the book's cover URLs from the copy to check a coverless front.
5. In both appearances, check the Share tab's **Book** row and list, and check that the other book's title and review stay unchanged everywhere.

### Check the dominoes' cameras

1. Turn **Reduce Motion** off, since with it on the finale cuts to the spread. Load the preview library, then launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsShelfMode pile -BookwormsSetUpDominoes YES -BookwormsDominoCamera follow -BookwormsDominoPush 10 -BookwormsDominoCameraLog YES`, adding `-BookwormsPileFrame year:2025` or `lifetime`. The push comes ten seconds after every book stands, once the establishing move, which takes at most nine seconds, has settled.
2. Record the run with `xcrun simctl io <device> recordVideo --codec=h264 <file>.mp4`, and stop it with Control-C once the heading shows over the gathered books. Record with nothing else running on the Mac, such as tests, a timing measurement, or another simulator: the video must show about 60 frames a second, or the last book's fall and the slam's 0.1-second shake can fall between frames.
3. Extract frames, such as `ffmpeg -i <file>.mp4 -vf fps=60 frame-%03d.png`, and check that the camera chases the front without jitter, swings smoothly round the turns, keeps the falling books and tearing cobwebs in view, and settles on the last book as it falls. Then check that the camera moves to the spread's view in one move with no hold, about where the overview stood, while the fallen chain lies still for a second, that every book then rises at once and hovers in view, that the books sink out of the cloud and glide into the spread at their own pace, several in the air at once, each landing after the books it rests on, never through each other, while the last book hovers beside the cloud, and that it then flies toward the camera, glides past it slowly, its cover square to it, and slams flat into its place with no held frame, blowing a soft, warm haze of dust out from under all its sides within the first frames as the camera jolts briefly, with no outline, separate grains, or puff edges, even in frames zoomed to twice their size, that thins as it spreads and is gone within 2 seconds without hiding the book's title, and that the spread's books hop a hair and settle without sliding apart, before the heading fades in. While the books fall, check that the chain runs on without pausing from the push to the last book and visibly speeds up in a long chain, and that the falling front stays near the middle of the view through every swing, push in, and the finale's first seconds.
4. Read `Library/Caches/Bookworms/domino-camera.csv` from the app's data container to measure the camera's speed, acceleration, turn rate, and roll from frame to frame, and the finale's move: from the frame where `finale` turns 0, the speed should rise without a pause, peak in the move's first half, and fall smoothly to 0 2 seconds in. The log ends once the books lie gathered and the camera has stopped, or after 14,400 frames. Its last columns give where the front's book shows across the view (`frontx`) and the part of the view's width that the recording's frames show (`shownl` to `shownr`, -1 before the push), as shares of the width. `domino-rig.txt` holds the path, the opening view, the cobwebs, and the books, so you can project the front's book through each logged pose to measure how much of the view's height it fills, the camera's elevation above it, and how often it shows between the bars, and replay the logged fronts through `DominoCameraRig` off the device to try a change before recording again. Repeat with `-BookwormsDominoCamera overview`.
5. To check switching cameras, launch without `-BookwormsDominoCamera`, and choose **Camera** in **Desk options** while the chain runs.
6. Repeat with `-BookwormsPileFrame lifetime` to check the worm's rows: the chain fills a rounded-top shape, narrowing a little toward the bottom, and the Follow camera swings round its shorter head rows as it does round the body's; a month's chain winds in even rows.

### Check the dominoes' setup

1. Load the preview library, start recording the Simulator with `xcrun simctl io <device> recordVideo --codec h264 <file>`, and launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsTab desk -BookwormsSetUpDominoes YES -BookwormsSetupLog YES`, adding `-BookwormsPileFrame month:2025-8` for a few books and `-BookwormsPileFrame lifetime` for the most.
2. Step through the frames from the menu choice to the standing chain, for example with `ffmpeg -vf fps=15`. The view should not jump as the books move across the table, and the camera should ease out to show the books and the chain. Each book should turn up onto its tail where it lies, perhaps stand there a moment while its way clears, hop upright, and drop straight into its place, one after another in finish order; no book should pass through another that moves or stands, apart from a book let go after waiting as long as it may, nor hang above its place, books lying on a book that turns up should slide off rather than fly, the cobwebs should fade in as the books behind them land, and the standing chain should not move as the setup ends.
3. Read `Library/Caches/Bookworms/domino-setup.csv`: each book's index in the chain, when it was due and when it started, how long its way was blocked (`held`), its arc's lift, the milliseconds its start took to plan, how long it stood on its tail before it hopped (`wait`), and 1 for a book let go regardless (`forced`); a row without a lift is a check that found the book's way blocked, with the milliseconds it took. The lines `still`, `offset`, and `cobwebs` give the seconds from the handover, once the dominoes have built the desk's books, until the books lay still, until their places were cleared, and until every cobweb hung; `repinned` counts the times a landed book was put back in its place, and `snap` gives the farthest any book lay from its place as the chain's physics started, which should be 0; `landed` gives the time the last book landed, in seconds from when the first book was due; and `ready` gives the seconds from the handover until the chain stands ready for the push. None of them counts the scenes' swap before the handover. Sum the milliseconds of the rows that share a start time for each frame's planning, and count those rows for each frame's starts, which should be at most the books due in it. For `lifetime`, also list the recording's frame times, for example with `ffprobe -show_entries frame=pts_time`, and check that no gap between frames longer than about 0.1 seconds falls from the menu choice until the books hop, or while they hop, and that no gap longer than about 30 milliseconds falls when the last book lands or when the lamp's pool settles over the chain 1.5 seconds later, apart from one gap of up to about 50 milliseconds when the recording's render callback goes on, about a tenth of a second after the chain stands (see [Dominoes](#dominoes)); the Simulator records a frame only when the display changes, so a gap there is a frame the app did not draw. Frames still drop at the menu choice, in two stretches: the scenes' swap, in which the dominoes build the books as rigid bodies and RealityKit takes them in, and SwiftUI's build of the dominoes' controls in place of the Desk scene's, which takes longest at the first setup after launch.
   Judge the gaps in an optimized build, such as the Debug configuration built with `SWIFT_OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL=-O`, which keeps the launch arguments; an unoptimized build spends many times as long in the app's own Swift code, such as framing the chain for the camera. Record with no log stream attached, such as `log stream`, which adds the cost of the frameworks' logging to the frames; read the app's log afterward with `log show` instead.
4. Let the debug pushes topple the chain, such as with `-BookwormsDominoPush 10`, and once the books have gathered, read `Library/Caches/Bookworms/domino-overlaps.csv`. Each frame of the setup and of the finale's gathering tests every pair of books' boxes, shrunk by 0.02 units on every side, and the log lists each overlap: the phase, `setup` or `finale`, the seconds into it, the two books' indices in the chain, and what each was doing. The last lines count each phase's frames, overlaps, and pairs of books. The finale lists few or none: books rising from where the desk's physics left them sunk into each other, and, in a long chain, books the planner let go after waiting as long as it may. The setup lists books still lying that the physics lets sink into each other as the books under them leave, books let go while their way is blocked, and books that hop past the corner of another between the schedule's checks, every 1/60 second. With `-BookwormsSetupLog YES`, the finale also writes `domino-gather.csv`: the gathering's budget, then for each book, in the spread's order, where it lies and its place, each as a position and a rotation's vector, and its size, so a gathering can be replayed through `DominoFinale.Gather` off the device.
5. Choose **Back to desk**, throw a book, and choose **Dominoes** again, then repeat with Reduce Motion on, where the books should stand at once.

### Check the Desk's tips

1. Load the preview library, then launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsTab desk -BookwormsTips reset`. About 10 seconds after the drop lands, the desk pauses, and **Pick up a book** points at a book near the middle of the view.
2. Launch again with `-BookwormsSetUpDominoes YES -BookwormsDominoPush 8` added. Once the chain stands and the camera rests, **Push the first book** points at the hand. At the push, the tip goes, and the red recording dot and the time show under the bar.
3. `-BookwormsTips show` shows every tip whatever its rules, such as **Look around the desk**, which otherwise waits for two throws.

### Check a dominoes video

1. Load the preview library, start recording the Simulator with `xcrun simctl io <device> recordVideo --codec h264 <file>`, then launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsTab desk -BookwormsSetUpDominoes YES -BookwormsDominoPush 3 -BookwormsDominoVideo YES`, adding `-BookwormsPileFrame month:2025-8` for a short chain and `-BookwormsPileFrame lifetime` for a long one. The push topples the chain and the books gather.
2. Once the app's log, in the `video` category, says how many frames and hits it recorded and how long the movie took to write ("Recorded N of M frames and H hits; the movie took S s to write"), wait until both `Library/Caches/Bookworms/domino-video-debug.mp4` and `domino-video-debug.png` show modification times after that line, since the copy follows the log, then copy them out of the app's data container (`xcrun simctl get_app_container booted gay.ian.Bookworms.iOS data`).
3. Check the MP4's format as [Check a video's format](#check-a-videos-format) describes: 1080 by 1920, at most 33 seconds, about 12 seconds for a month's chain. The PNG is 1080 by 1920 in sRGB.
4. Extract frames, such as `ffmpeg -i domino-video-debug.mp4 -vf fps=1 frame-%03d.png`, and check that the clip opens at the push, that a long chain falls faster than live and the finale at live speed, that the colors match the screen recording, that the heading fades in where the screen shows it and holds to the end, that the last 3 seconds show the last frame still, which matches the PNG, and that the credit shows at the bottom edge throughout.
5. Decode the sound with `ffmpeg -i domino-video-debug.mp4 -map 0:a out.wav` and check that the knocks start with the frames where books strike and land and come from their side of the picture, and that the last book's deep knock lands with the frame where it slams into the spread.
6. Tap **Share**, then **Video**: the share sheet offers the MP4 by the run's name, such as "Dominoes August 2025", and **Copy** shows a "Copied" toast. **Image** offers the PNG. To check **Image** without a recording, switch to Library while the chain falls and back: **Share** then says that a video takes a whole run and offers only **Image**.
7. On a Mac, check the frame. Build the Debug app unsigned with `xcodebuild -project Bookworms.xcodeproj -scheme Bookworms -destination 'generic/platform=macOS' CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build`. It runs outside the sandbox, so it reads and writes `~/Library/Caches/Bookworms`; to keep a scratch folder instead, launch its executable with `CFFIXED_USER_HOME` set to that folder, and copy `.local/preview` to `Library/Caches/Bookworms/preview` inside it. Preferences, including the window's saved frame, still go to `~/Library/Preferences` under the app's bundle ID, so give a copy of the app a scratch bundle ID (`/usr/libexec/PlistBuddy -c "Set :CFBundleIdentifier <scratch ID>" Bookworms.app/Contents/Info.plist`, then `codesign --force --deep -s - Bookworms.app`), which also marks the copy with a grayscale Dock icon and **Test copy** in its title bars (see [Run locally](#run-locally)), and afterward delete `~/Library/Preferences/<scratch ID>.plist` and the copy's saved state and caches, `$(getconf DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR)<scratch ID>.savedState` and `$(getconf DARWIN_USER_CACHE_DIR)<scratch ID>`. Launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsTab desk -BookwormsSetUpDominoes YES -BookwormsDominoPush 10 -BookwormsDominoVideo YES -BookwormsDominoCameraLog YES -ApplePersistenceIgnoreState YES`, adding `-BookwormsPileFrame year:2025` or `lifetime`, once with `-BookwormsDominoCamera overview` and once with `follow`. The push comes after the Follow camera's establishing move has settled. A new bundle ID without restored state opens the default window, 1180 by 820 points, where the frame shows about 40 percent of the view's width. In the window, the chain should set up, stand, fall, and gather inside that 9:16 part of the view, with the table on either side darkened from the setup on, and the spread and the heading should fill it. Check that the video opens on the hand and the first book; that every book falls inside the frame, with Overview showing the whole chain and Follow keeping the front near the frame's middle; and that it ends on the gathered books, every one of them inside the frame and the PNG, with the last book springing up inside the frame, and the heading and the credit holding still. In `domino-camera.csv`, `shownl` and `shownr` give the frame's edges and stay put, and `frontx` should lie between them while the books fall (`fallen` is 0 and `shownl` is not -1). Repeat once with `-BookwormsDominoPush 0`, which pushes while the recording arms: the video should still open on the first book.

### Check a video's format

Run `ffprobe -show_streams -show_format <file>.mp4` and check for two streams: `h264` video with profile `High`, `pix_fmt=yuv420p`, `color_primaries=bt709`, 30 frames per second, and `aac` audio with profile `LC`, `sample_rate=48000`, and 2 channels, of the same length, in a `mov,mp4` file whose brand is `mp42`. `ffprobe -v trace <file>.mp4 2>&1 | grep -m 2 -o "type:'m[od][oa][vt]'"` lists `moov` before `mdat` when the index is at the front.

### Check the dominoes' sounds and haptics

The Simulator plays no haptics and its audio cannot be heard, so the log counts what a device plays.

1. Load the preview library, then launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsTab desk -BookwormsSetUpDominoes YES -BookwormsDominoPush 3 -BookwormsImpactLog YES`.
2. Once the chain has fallen, read `Library/Caches/Bookworms/impact-log.csv` from the app's data container, written every 20 hits: each hit's clock, impulse, mass, strength, whether it played a tap and a sound, and the knock's version, from 0 to 7, or 8 for the finale's deep knock, which also writes the file. Neighboring knocks are never the same version. A 39-book chain logs about 220 hits, of which about 85 sound and 60 tap; the Desk scene's drops log the same way.
3. On an iPhone, push the chain and listen for a low knock at every strike and landing, louder for a flat landing and varying slightly from hit to hit, and feel a tap with each. As the camera comes to rest at the end, the last book's slam should knock deeper and tap harder than any other landing. Then check that the Desk scene's drops knock and tap the same way.

### Check the friends' templates

Use fictional data only: the preview library holds real reading history, so make a fictional preview folder for images you keep or show.

1. Write a fictional `library-v1.json` (a Hardcover source with rated, finished books and an empty `spine-designs-v1.json`) into the app's `Library/Caches/Bookworms/<folder>`.
2. Launch with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder <folder> -BookwormsShareFriends YES -BookwormsShareTemplate tasteMatch -BookwormsShareBatch "<entries>"`, where a fifth part of an entry names a fictional reader: `robinvale` (mostly agrees, a few books in common), `samokafor` (mostly disagrees), `marisol_ow` (a long name), or `junpark` (too few books for a score). For example: `"tasteMatch,story,lifetime,threeD,robinvale tasteMatch,square,lifetime,flat,samokafor bookSwap,portrait,lifetime,threeD,samokafor hotTakes,story,year:2026,threeD,samokafor"`. The Share tab writes each entry to `Library/Caches/Bookworms/share-debug-<entry>.png`.
3. Check every size for clipped or truncated text, the invitation above the credit, and the empty states, for example with `junpark` and Taste Match. Each fictional reader gives 5 stars to the first unrated book with `"statusID":1` (Want to Read) in the library, so Book Swap shows "Already on my list" under that pick when the library has one. Book Swap with `junpark`, who shares the fewest books and so has picks on both sides, is the tightest layout: on every size and cover style, check that the header and the invitation stay inside the margins, which on a Story keep them out of the 250-pixel strips.
4. Seesaw renders from a RealityKit scene, so give it a few seconds per entry: for example `"seesaw,story,year:2026,threeD,robinvale seesaw,square,lifetime,threeD,junpark"`. Check that the plank tips toward the side whose counts show more pages, that its low end rests on the table, and that the names and counts sit under their own ends. To preview with your real friends instead, add `-BookwormsShareFriendsFile <path>` with a copy of a signed-in device's `Library/Caches/Bookworms/social-snapshot.json`; those images hold other readers' real reading history, so never publish them.

### Check the empty states

1. On a Simulator without an iCloud account, launch without `-BookwormsPreviewFolder`, adding `-BookwormsOfferedHardcover NO` to check that the Hardcover sheet opens over the Share tab, with a prominent **Connect Hardcover**. Close it and relaunch without the argument: the sheet stays closed. The Share tab shows the blotter, **No library yet** with the iCloud error, **Connect Hardcover**, and **Try again**; add `-BookwormsTab library` or `desk` to check the empty bookcase and the desk still.
2. Launch with `-BookwormsLibraryStatus downloading`, `failed`, or `reconnect` to check the signed-in states. `downloading` shows "200 of 412 books" and a half-full shelf. The argument also sets the signs in [Where the account shows](#where-the-account-shows), over a preview library too: `signedOut` and `reconnect` show the iPhone's words and the Mac's row, `reconnect` the iPhone's bar, and `failed` the badge or the mark. `-BookwormsAccountName <name>` gives the Mac's account row a username, which a preview library lacks.
3. Add `-BookwormsShowSpines YES -BookwormsShowAccount YES -BookwormsStubSignIn YES` to check the library card, in **Settings** on the iPhone.

### Check the Mac toolbar for a layout loop

Where a Mac window leaves its toolbar just too narrow for its items, AppKit can lay out the toolbar again and again at full CPU while nothing on screen changes, deciding anew on each pass whether to squeeze an item or move items into the overflow (**»**) menu. The widths that do this depend on everything in the title bar: the section, the time frame button's title and count, and the **Test copy** label, which only test copies show. So on the Mac, Desk's options menu and the dominoes' **Share** menu take a fixed size (`DeskBar.options`), and a check sweeps every width:

1. Build a Debug Mac copy with a scratch bundle ID, signed ad hoc and without entitlements: `xcodebuild -project Bookworms.xcodeproj -scheme Bookworms -configuration Debug -destination 'platform=macOS,arch=arm64' -derivedDataPath <scratch>/dd PRODUCT_BUNDLE_IDENTIFIER=<scratch ID> CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY=- CODE_SIGN_STYLE=Manual DEVELOPMENT_TEAM= CODE_SIGN_ENTITLEMENTS= build`. It reads a scratch copy of a preview folder, such as the example profile's export. Afterward, delete its preferences, saved state, and caches as step 7 of [Check a dominoes video](#check-a-dominoes-video) describes.
2. Launch `<scratch>/dd/Build/Products/Debug/Bookworms.app/Contents/MacOS/Bookworms -BookwormsPreviewFolder <folder> -ApplePersistenceIgnoreState YES -BookwormsTab desk -BookwormsPileFrame lifetime -GeneratesWhileCharging NO -BookwormsWindowSweep 780,1200`. `-GeneratesWhileCharging NO` keeps spine generation from starting: the sweep records the whole app's CPU, and a generation pass reads a cover about every 45 seconds, which raises about five widths in a row to well over 100 percent wherever the sweep happens to be. Thirty seconds after launch, once the drop rests, the main window widens one point at a time from 780 points, its minimum, to 1,200, which takes about 11 minutes. `~/Library/Caches/Bookworms/window-sweep.csv` records each width and the app's CPU use there, as a percentage of one core.
3. Repeat with `-BookwormsPileFrame year:2025` and `month:2025-8`, and with `-BookwormsTab library`. The dominoes' **Share** shows only once a run finishes, so for the dominoes add `-BookwormsSetUpDominoes YES -BookwormsDominoPush 3` and a third value that starts the sweep after the run, such as `780,1200,180`.
4. An idle window records a few percent at every width, and Desk records more, up to about 100 percent in a Debug copy, because its scene draws again after each resize; a width that stays near 100 percent on the other tabs, or stands well above its neighbors on Desk, is a loop. A raised run of widths that moves when you sweep again from another width follows the clock, not the width, and is not a loop. To confirm one, launch at that width without the sweep, adding `-"NSWindow Frame main" "100 100 <width> 820 0 0 1512 949"`, which stands in for the window's saved frame: its x, y, width, and height, then the screen's. After 40 seconds, `ps -o %cpu= -p <pid>` reads about 100 for a loop. `sample <pid> 3 -file loop.sample`, then `grep -c "NSToolbarView layout" loop.sample`, counts the toolbar's layout frames, which an idle app doesn't have.

Known issue: in a test copy with the example profile, Library loops at 798 to 800 points wide, where **View as** and the options menu sit in the overflow menu and the search field takes the room. A copy without the **Test copy** label showed no loop on Library.

### Check spine fonts with a batch

`script/run_mac.sh --spine-batch <books.json>` designs a list of books with the Mac app and records what each design read and chose, without saving a design. `books.json` is an array of `CloudBook` records. `--batch-tier <pcc|backup|onDevice|local>` picks the model tier; without it, the best available tier designs. It combines with `--no-pcc`.

The script compiles with `SPINE_BATCH`, signs the build as `script/run_mac.sh` signs the installed app, copies it to `.local/spine-batch/Bookworms-batch.app`, copies the preview library and the books file into the app's container, and launches that copy beside any running Bookworms with `-BookwormsPreviewFolder preview -BookwormsSpineBatch spine-batch-books.json`. The installed `~/Applications/Bookworms.app` stays as it is. The batch build shares the installed app's container and Keychain items because it has the same bundle ID, team, and entitlements, so it designs with your saved provider key. It shows only a progress window and quits when the batch ends; the script then copies the run's folder to `.local/spine-batch/` and deletes the batch build. Each run's folder holds `results.json`, with each book's design, including its `choice`, or its error, plus each cover as the design measured it (`cover-<id>.png`) and the title line the model read (`crop-<id>.png`), unless several OCR lines on the cover share its text. Debug builds accept the same launch arguments.

A batch runs only in a preview launch, so it reads no iCloud data, and it saves nothing: designs stay in memory, and scheduled runs don't start during it (`SpineBatch`). Model requests are real: Private Cloud Compute's count against the daily limit, and a provider's are billed to your API account. The batch stops at the first daily limit, lasting rejection, rate limit, outage, or unreachable endpoint.

### Test Hardcover sign-in

A real sign-in needs a person to approve it in a browser, so the unit tests use canned responses. To check it on a Simulator or iPhone:

1. Launch without `-BookwormsPreviewFolder` or `-BookwormsStubSignIn`, open **Spines**, and tap **Settings** (on the Mac, select **Connect Hardcover** in the sidebar). On the iPhone, check that **Book details** follows the account, and that **Connect Hardcover** on an empty **Share**, **Library**, or **Desk** tab opens the sheet as **Hardcover**, with only the account.
2. Tap **Connect Hardcover**, then **Open Hardcover**. Sign in to Hardcover in Safari, check that the code is filled in or paste it, and allow Bookworms.
3. Within a few seconds Safari closes, the sheet shows your bookplate and a sync in progress, the empty tabs count the books as they arrive, and the Library tab then shows your Hardcover library.
4. Tap **Sync now** and check that **Last synced** updates. Tap **Sign out** and check that Library shows the iCloud archive's library, which after the sync can be the one this device saved, or its empty state.

To check **Reconnect**, revoke Bookworms in Hardcover's account settings, then tap **Sync now**.

## iCloud contract

No device owns the `library-v1` archive: the Apple TV app and this app each write the Hardcover snapshots they download (see the archive's contract below). The Apple TV app, from builds that include the spine companion work, writes the `SpineWorklistV1` record. This app writes one `SpineDesignV1` record per book, a `CoverChoiceV1` or `SeriesLinkV1` record for each book whose cover or series the reader chose, a `TopFivePinsV1` record for the account's Top Five pins, and a `SeriesLookV1` record per series look, which the Apple TV can also write. Record names and JSON fields are defined in `Bookworms/Models/CloudContract.swift`, and the tests pin the same digests as the Apple TV tests. Designs add optional `fontFamily`, `fontPostScriptName`, `instanceWeight`, `provenance`, and `choice` fields; older readers ignore them. `choice` records, for review, the OCR text of the measured title line (`titleLine`), the model's reading of the lettering (`titleText`, `titleCase`, `making`, `category`, `subclass`, `themes`, `moods`, `weight`, `width`, and `note`, absent for local defaults), and up to six families the design tried, best first (`SpineChoice`). Generation and the series rules never read it: recoloring keeps it, and runs keep, move, and replace designs as they would without it. The font picker lists its families first (see [Spine font](#spine-font)). The app never shows Impact or a face that declares a slant.
