A saved design or series look whose family or PostScript name the type rules block shows in an allowed family instead, in the upright face nearest the design's weight: Impact in Bebas Neue, Bangers in Bungee, Shrikhand in Changa One, a slanted or leaning script, such as Dancing Script, Pacifico, Kalam, Caveat, Permanent Marker, or Shadows Into Light, in Patrick Hand, an upright hand-drawn face, and an italic, oblique, or slanted variant, such as Lora Italic or `Lora-BoldIt`, in its upright family. Any other blocked family uses the design's system typeface. A downloaded face with the italic trait or a slant never renders. The stored record stays unchanged (`SpineFamily.uprightReplacement(for:)`, `SpineDesignV1.withAllowedFont`). A design or look without a family whose `fontPostScriptName` names an upright face installed on the device, such as Georgia-Bold in a look from the Apple TV, draws in that face; otherwise it uses its system typeface.

`provenance` records how a design was made, in space-separated tokens of at most 80 characters in all. A `model:` token names the model tier that designed it (`SpineDesignV1.tier`): `model:<provider>/<model>` for a backup model, such as `model:anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5`, `model:apple/on-device` for Apple's on-device model, or `model:local` for local defaults. Private Cloud Compute's designs record no model token. Without one, the generator decides (`SpineDesignV1.modelToken`): a design from any version of this app's generator, such as `ios-pcc-v1` or `ios-pcc-v2`, reads as Private Cloud Compute, one from any version of the retired Mac companion's, `mac-foundation-models-v1` or `mac-foundation-models-v2`, as Apple's on-device model, and any other, such as the design of a `tvos-local-v1` look, as local defaults. So a new generator version changes no design's tier. The companion used no other model, so a `model:local` token with its generator, which builds that read `mac-foundation-models-v1` as local defaults wrote into looks made from its designs, also reads as Apple's on-device model. Copies those builds made of such a look record `model:local` with this app's generator and still read as local defaults: a book that draws the look gets a copy of the look as it is now, which records `model:apple/on-device`, at its next check, and any other book's copy gives way to Apple's on-device model or a better tier. `font:system` marks a local-defaults design set in its style's system typeface because its Google Fonts family couldn't download; it ranks below the tier's other designs, so a later run designs the book again (`SpineDesignV1.awaitsFont`). A copy of a look or of another book's design keeps its source's model token and `font:system` token. `series-reuse:<book ID>` marks a design copied from the series' look, naming the look's source book, or from another book's design when the run saved it; the app draws a series with its look or current source's design either way (see [Series designs](#series-designs)). `generated-colors:<background>,<text>,<accent>` marks colors the reader chose (see [Spine colors](#spine-colors)) and holds the generator's colors, each as six uppercase hexadecimal digits, such as `series-reuse:12 generated-colors:1A2B3C,F4E9D0,C08040`; `reader-font` marks a font the reader chose (see [Spine font](#spine-font)), which the design's `fontFamily`, `fontPostScriptName`, `instanceWeight`, and `typeface` name; choosing a font also removes `font:system`. When the tokens would exceed 80 characters, the colors and font tokens stay, the others but the model token go first, and the model token is shortened last, so the design keeps its tier. Readers validate `provenance` only as a single line of 1 to 80 characters.

- **Font choice.** A design set in the reader's font replaces the font fields and adds `reader-font` in the same record, keeping the colors and every other field, including `generatedAt`. Like a recoloring, it applies to the book's design as iCloud holds it, even one another device redesigned or recolored meantime, and each of the two edits keeps the other's token. Only a book without a saved design refuses it (`CompanionError.designChanged`).
- **Recoloring.** A recolored design replaces the colors in the same record and keeps every other field, including `generatedAt`. It applies to the book's design as iCloud holds it, so the reader's colors also land on a redesign or a font change that another device saved meantime. Restoring the generator's colors removes the token.
- **Generation.** A run keeps a design with the colors or font token, whatever generator or tier made it, until the reader chooses another cover whose image differs, and keeps any other design that the run's tier doesn't outrank: one from Private Cloud Compute or a backup model at any tier, and one from the run's tier or a better one (`SpineDesignV1.action(workItemHash:coverHash:readerChoseCover:runTier:drawsSeriesLook:upgradesSeriesLook:)`). When the book's work-item hash changes, the run saves the kept design under the new work-item hash, with the same cover hash, colors, font, generator version, `provenance`, and `generatedAt`, because the Apple TV ignores a design whose work-item hash does not match the book, provided the design's font letters the book's title and author; otherwise the record stays as it is. A new image behind the book's default cover changes no record. The one exception is a sync's sharper default cover, which has a new URL: it redesigns a book's own design with the generator's colors and font (see [Spine generation](#spine-generation)). A design with the generator's colors whose cover hash differs from the cover the reader chose, downloaded at every check, is redesigned from it. A save that finds a saved design of the book, from any generator and under any hashes, leaves it when the new design doesn't outrank it, as for a design from Private Cloud Compute or a backup model or one from the new design's tier or a better one, or when it has the reader's colors or font (`SpineDesignV1.outlasts(_:replacing:)`). So the first device to save a design at a tier keeps it, the first capable model's design stays, and a save decided from an older read, or under another device's library snapshot, replaces no design it would keep now. A series copy with the generator's colors and font is the exception, because a copy follows its group's look: a newer copy of the same work replaces it at its tier or a better one, and a copy without a model token gives way to any newer copy of its work. A design from a better tier replaces one from Apple's on-device model or local defaults, and the saved design itself moved to a new work-item hash, with its colors, font, and `provenance` as they are now, replaces it, so a move decided before you recolored the design or chose its font leaves your edit, and a check a day later moves the edited design. A new copy of a series' look that replaces an older copy, moved to a new work-item hash or not, saves only while the book's saved design is still the one the check read (`SpineStore.save(_:replacing:ifUnchangedFrom:)`), so colors or a font you chose while the check waited for a font download stay, and a check a day later moves the edited design. A design that **Redesign spine** or **Redesign spines** asked for, or one of the cover the reader chose, replaces a design under other hashes or an older one of the same work. A saved design whose `version` isn't 1, such as a newer build's, is neither read nor replaced, even by a redesign you asked for: the run stops with "Update this app to read the iCloud data." A malformed version-1 record counts as no design.

This app also writes its Hardcover library into the `library-v1` archive, which the Apple TV app reads and writes too:

- **Location.** The record named `library-v1`, of type `LibraryArchive`, in the default zone of the private database. Its `archive` asset holds JSON: `version` (`1`), `sources`, `designs` (the Apple TV's own spine styles by book ID, which this app doesn't read), and an optional `merges` (see [One Hardcover account in iCloud](#one-hardcover-account-in-icloud)). Each entry of `sources` holds `source` (`hardcover` or `cwa`), `accountID`, `syncedAt` (when its writer finished the download, in the same date encoding as cover choices), `books`, an optional `series`, each with `id`, `name`, and `books`, and an optional `username`, the account's Hardcover username.
- **Reads.** The app reads an asset of at most 50 MB and rejects a `version` other than 1. An account's snapshot is fresh for 24 hours after its `syncedAt`. Each source and account counts alone, and the record's modification date never counts.
- **Writes.** After each Hardcover download, the app fetches the record, or starts a new one, edits its JSON, and saves with `ifServerRecordUnchanged`, retrying up to three times on a conflict (`CloudLibraryArchive.splice(_:into:)`). It replaces the newest entry for its own source and account only when its `syncedAt` is strictly newer than every such entry, appends one when there is none, and never removes one. Every other key, snapshot, and `designs` stays as it was, and entries sort by source and account ID, as the Apple TV sorts them. A snapshot without `series` or `username` keeps the stored entry's. Books leave out `chosenCoverURL` and `seriesLink`, and carry the two fields only the Apple TV stores: `sources`, always `["hardcover"]`, and `isOwned`, kept from the same book in the stored entry. The app also refuses an entry whose stored `syncedAt` isn't a number. A new archive starts with `version` 1 and an empty `designs`, which the Apple TV needs to decode it. The app never writes over an archive with another version, or over a record without a readable asset.
- **Compatibility.** The Apple TV decodes every snapshot into its own book type and encodes it again whenever it uploads, so a book or snapshot field must exist in both apps; Apple TV builds without `review` drop it. The tests decode the spliced JSON through a copy of the Apple TV's archive types.

This app also writes one `CoverChoiceV1` record for each book whose cover the reader changed:

- **Location.** Record type `CoverChoiceV1` in the default zone of the private database of `iCloud.gay.ian.Bookworms`.
- **Record name.** `cover-choice-v1-` followed by the lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 of `<accountID>:<bookID>`, the same digest as design record names. For account `42` and book `7`, the name is `cover-choice-v1-755dac3729029e2e2976249c4e03b160c699ef71f296c346e22183dc23503c33`.
- **Payload.** A `payload` field of type Bytes holds JSON: `version` (`1`), `accountID` (string), `bookID` (integer), `coverURL` (string, an HTTPS image URL), `editionID` (integer, the Hardcover edition; absent when the cover belongs to no edition), and `chosenAt` (number, `JSONEncoder`'s default date encoding: seconds since 1 January 2001, which can be fractional). For example: `{"accountID":"42","bookID":7,"chosenAt":800000000,"coverURL":"https://covers.example.com/7/2.jpg","editionID":31,"version":1}`.
- **Validation.** Readers ignore a payload unless `version` is 1, `accountID` has 1 to 512 characters, `bookID` is positive, `coverURL` passes `CoverURLPolicy` (HTTPS, no credentials, port, or fragment, and no local or IP-address host), `editionID` is positive when present, and the account and book match the record name.
- **Writes.** Choosing the default cover deletes the record. A save fetches the record, returns early when it already names the same cover and edition, and otherwise saves with `ifServerRecordUnchanged`, retrying up to three times on a conflict, like designs.
- **Use.** The chosen cover goes first among the book's covers for display and generation, followed by the default covers as fallbacks. It never enters `workItemHash`, so designs keep matching the Apple TV's hash; a design's `coverHash` is the SHA-256 of the chosen cover's bytes.

Books carry every finished read's date in an optional `finishedDates` list, newest first, in this app's Hardcover cache, in the snapshots it saves to `library-v1`, and in the snapshots of Apple TV builds that download it. Each entry is a `yyyy-MM-dd`-prefixed string, as `finished` is, and `finished` equals the first entry. A book without the list, as in older snapshots, or with an empty one, has one known read when it has `finished` (`CloudBook.readCount`). Readers that predate the list ignore it, and it doesn't enter `workItemHash`. For example: `{"finished":"2026-02-01","finishedDates":["2026-02-01","2019-07-04"]}`.

Books carry their Hardcover edition's format in an optional `editionFormat` string, Hardcover's `editions.edition_format` as Hardcover records it, such as `Hardcover` or `Mass Market Paperback`, in this app's Hardcover cache, in the snapshots it saves to `library-v1`, and in the snapshots of Apple TV builds that download it. It comes from the first of the newest finished read's edition and the library entry's edition whose format names a hardcover or paperback, else from the same edition as `format` and `pages`, and is absent without an edition. Both apps pick it with the same keywords, so a newer audiobook or ebook read doesn't hide a print edition on the library entry. Only this app reads it, for a binding that no view draws (`CloudBook.likelyBinding`); every book draws as a hardcover. An Apple TV build without the field drops it when it uploads. Readers that predate the field ignore it, and it doesn't enter `workItemHash`. For example: `{"editionFormat":"Paperback","format":"Read"}`.

Books carry Hardcover's shelf in an optional `statusID` integer, Hardcover's `user_books.status_id`, such as `1` for **Want to Read**, `2` for **Currently Reading**, and `3` for **Read**, in this app's Hardcover cache, in the snapshots it saves to `library-v1`, and in the snapshots of Apple TV builds that download it. Neither app saves a book on **Ignored** (`6`). A book without the field, as in older snapshots, has no known shelf; `isRead` and `isReading` still mark read books and current reads. Readers that predate the field ignore it, and it doesn't enter `workItemHash`. For example: `{"isRead":true,"statusID":3}`.

A book on **Did Not Finish** (`5`) also carries `dropped`, a `yyyy-MM-dd` day in the reader's time zone. Hardcover's reading journal, which logs shelf changes, is closed to the apps' scopes, so the first sync that finds a book on that shelf takes the day of its entry's `updated_at`, and later syncs keep that day while the book stays there. A book dropped long before its first sync, or edited again before it, gets a later day than its real drop. Both apps download it with the library and save it to `library-v1`. Readers that predate the field ignore it, and it doesn't enter `workItemHash`. For example: `{"statusID":5,"dropped":"2025-08-14"}`.

Books carry their series in an optional `seriesMemberships` list, in this app's Hardcover cache, in the snapshots it saves to `library-v1`, and in the snapshots of Apple TV builds that download it. Each entry holds `seriesID` (integer), `name` (string, trimmed and never empty), `position` (number; absent when the book has no place in the series), `featured` and `compilation` (Booleans), and `size` (integer, Hardcover's `primary_books_count`, else `books_count`; absent when Hardcover gives neither). For example: `{"compilation":false,"featured":true,"name":"Discworld","position":8,"seriesID":1018,"size":41}`. A book without the list groups by `seriesID`. `seriesID`, `seriesPosition`, and `seriesName` keep naming Hardcover's featured series. None of these enter `workItemHash`.

Each look group has at most one `SeriesLookV1` record (see [Series designs](#series-designs)):

- **Location.** Record type `SeriesLookV1` in the same zone and database as designs.
- **Record name.** `series-look-v1-` followed by the lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 of `<accountID>:<seriesID>:<group>`, where `group` joins the Discworld group (empty outside `SeriesDesigns.subseries`) and the first author's key, folded to lowercase without diacritics, spaces, or punctuation, with `|`. For account `42`, series `1018`, and group `City Watch|terrypratchett`, the name is `series-look-v1-f6b8bd60e3d149603964e4149bb554dfad824022ea2eb99f8ca02e5708be48b1`.
- **Payload.** A `payload` field of type Bytes holds JSON: `version` (`1`), `accountID`, `seriesID`, `group`, `sourceBookID` (the book whose design or look it was made from), `origin` (the generator it was made from, such as `ios-pcc-v2`, or `tvos-local-v1` for a look the Apple TV made from Book Wall's own look), `createdAt`, `editedAt` (absent until the reader edits the look, or equal to `createdAt` for a look made from a design whose colors or font the reader chose), `remadeAt` (absent until a redesign or an upgrade makes the look again from a new design, which keeps `createdAt`), the design's `backgroundHex`, `foregroundHex`, `accentHex`, `typeface`, `weight`, `uppercase`, `fontFamily`, `fontPostScriptName`, and `instanceWeight`, and an optional `provenance` that holds the design's model token, such as `model:apple/on-device`, followed by `font:system` when the design awaits its font, or by `reader-font` once the reader chose the look's font, and nothing else; the app reads only those tokens, whichever device wrote the record, and a book that draws the look copies no `reader-font` token. A look without a model token reads by its `origin`, as a design does by its generator (`SeriesLookV1.tier`). A look from this app's generator saved before looks carried `provenance`, such as an `ios-pcc-v1` look, therefore reads as Private Cloud Compute whatever model made it, so no run upgrades it; **Redesign spines** remakes it. Older readers ignore `provenance`. Dates use the same encoding as cover choices.
- **Validation.** Readers ignore a payload unless `version` is 1, `accountID` has 1 to 512 characters, `seriesID` and `sourceBookID` are positive, `group` has at most 200 characters, `origin` is one line of 1 to 80 characters and is not blank, the colors and type pass the design rules (uppercase `#RRGGBB` colors with text contrast of at least 4.5:1, one of the four typefaces, a weight of 400, 500, 600, or 700, an instance weight in hundreds from 100 to 900, and font names on one line of at most 80 characters), and the account and group match the record name.
- **Writes.** Creating a look saves a new record with `ifServerRecordUnchanged`. When the record already exists, another device decided the look first: the save fails, and the app fetches and uses that look instead, or upgrades it when it comes from Apple's on-device model or local defaults, the run's tier is better, and this book is its upgrade source. Upgrading a look from a better tier's design, or from a design whose font downloaded when the look awaits its font, keeps `createdAt` and sets `remadeAt`, and saves over the record only while the saved look is still unedited and outranked that way; otherwise the app keeps the saved look, such as one the reader recolored or another device upgraded first. A look whose `version` isn't 1 is never upgraded. Recoloring a look replaces its colors and sets `editedAt`, keeping `origin`. Choosing its font replaces `typeface`, `fontFamily`, `fontPostScriptName`, and `instanceWeight`, puts `reader-font` in place of any `font:system`, and sets `editedAt`. Either edit applies only its fields to the look iCloud holds when it saves (`SeriesLookEdit`, `SpineStore.saveReaderEdit(_:at:to:)`), so another device's edit of the other field stays, and a look another device remade in the meantime takes the edit. Only a group without a look refuses it: the app then reloads and applies the edit once more to what the book draws then, and says another device is changing the spine only if that fails too. A requested redesign replaces the look's colors and type, `sourceBookID`, `origin`, and `provenance` with the new design's and sets `editedAt` and `remadeAt`, saving over the record, the last writer winning. Upgrades, recolorings, and redesigns retry up to three times on a conflict.
- **Use.** A book that draws the look draws it as its own design, with the look's `origin` as the generator, its `provenance`, and the work-item and cover hashes of the book's own saved design. The record never enters `workItemHash`.

This app also writes one `SeriesLinkV1` record for each book whose series choice the reader changed. The Apple TV app writes the same record when the reader picks a series in its book details, keeping the book's `ownLook`:

- **Location.** Record type `SeriesLinkV1` in the same zone and database as designs.
- **Record name.** `series-link-v1-` followed by the lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 of `<accountID>:<bookID>`. For account `42` and book `123`, the name is `series-link-v1-54bad7da96754d6c526b9e97c7ea7ee0fe033f6938e9a9a6dabd91758a7bb198`.
- **Payload.** A `payload` field of type Bytes holds JSON: `version` (`1`), `accountID`, `bookID`, `linkedSeriesID` (the series whose name its details show and, unless `ownLook` is set, whose look the book draws; absent for its default series), `standsAlone` (the book draws no series look and its details name no series), `ownLook` (the book draws its own design, from **Changes apply to** > **This book only**), and `chosenAt`, with the same date encoding as cover choices.
- **Validation.** Readers ignore a payload unless `version` is 1, `accountID` has 1 to 512 characters, `bookID` is positive, `linkedSeriesID` is positive when present, and the account and book match the record name.
- **Writes.** A save fetches the record and replaces only the choices you changed on this device, the series (a linked series or `standsAlone`) or `ownLook`, so the other choice stays as the record holds it, such as one another device saved since this device last loaded the library (`SeriesLinkV1.merged(over:changing:)`). It saves with `ifServerRecordUnchanged`, retrying up to three times on a conflict, like cover choices. A link that then sets none of a linked series, `standsAlone`, and `ownLook` deletes the record. A CloudKit delete doesn't check the record's change tag, so a choice that another device saves between the fetch and the delete is lost.
- **Use.** The app applies the link when it loads work or the library, before it groups books into looks. The record never enters `workItemHash`.

This app also writes one `TopFivePinsV1` record per account for the books pinned to Top Five (see [Share](#share)). The Apple TV doesn't read it:

- **Location.** Record type `TopFivePinsV1` in the same zone and database as designs.
- **Record name.** `top-five-pins-v1-` followed by the lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 of `<accountID>`. For account `42`, the name is `top-five-pins-v1-73475cb40a568e8da8a045ced110137e159f890ac4da883b6b17dc651b3a8049`.
- **Payload.** A `payload` field of type Bytes holds JSON: `version` (`1`), `accountID`, `bookIDs` (up to five Hardcover book IDs, in pin order; empty once every book is unpinned), and `pinnedAt`, with the same date encoding as cover choices. For example: `{"accountID":"42","bookIDs":[7,3],"pinnedAt":800000000,"version":1}`.
- **Validation.** Readers ignore a payload unless `version` is 1, `accountID` has 1 to 512 characters, `bookIDs` holds at most five distinct positive IDs, and the account matches the record name.
- **Writes.** The app keeps the pins in its user defaults so a pin shows at once, with the pins as iCloud last confirmed them. After each pin or unpin and after each reload, it compares the two (`AppModel.syncTopFivePins()`). When they match, the record's pins replace this device's. When they differ, or iCloud never confirmed pins on this device, a save fetches the record and changes only the pins added or removed on this device since the last confirmation, so a pin another device saved meanwhile stays; added pins follow the saved ones, and this device's pins keep this device's order. Over five, the newest saved pins this device lacks give way, so a new pin stays even when the record is full of pins this device hides (`TopFivePinsV1.merged(over:changing:)`). It saves with `ifServerRecordUnchanged`, retrying up to three times on a conflict, like series links. The record is never deleted. When the record is missing or unreadable but this device has confirmed pins, such as after a switch of iCloud account or between the Development and Production environments, the app saves this device's pins as the record instead of clearing them, including when the sync that finds it missing is saving a pin or unpin. Syncs run one at a time. A pin changed during a sync is applied over the pins the sync kept, and the next sync saves it. When the library or the pins change, **Share** removes books this device can't draw from both copies in its user defaults, so no sync saves that as an unpin. Showing another Hardcover account's library drops the pins it finds, and its first sync shows that account's record, so one account's pins never reach another's. A launch with `-TopFivePins` or `-BookwormsPreviewFolder` neither syncs nor removes pins, though a pin you tap there still changes the saved pins, and the next ordinary launch syncs it.
- **Use.** Top Five's pins and the **Pinned books** list read the user defaults. The record never enters `workItemHash`.

Current limits:

- Apple TV builds that include the companion-designs work cache companion designs and draw them on Book Wall while `BookPresentation.showsCompanionSpines` is on, and show chosen covers first while `BookPresentation.showsCompanionCovers` is on, both with the Apple TV's **iCloud storage** on. Only Apple TV builds that include the series-look work read `SeriesLinkV1` records and create and draw `SeriesLookV1` records. Apple TV 1.2.0 and later include both; Apple TV 1.1.0 and earlier ignore designs, looks, and choices and show the default covers.
- A development-signed build uses the CloudKit Development environment. It does not see data that App Store or TestFlight Apple TV builds write to Production.
- The [release workflow](docs/RELEASING.md) lists the App Store Connect, signing, production schema, App Review, and Production iCloud steps for each release.

Before a device asks a model for a book's design, it saves one `SpineClaimV1` record for the book (see [Spine generation](#spine-generation)). The Apple TV doesn't read it:

- **Location.** Record type `SpineClaimV1` in the same zone and database as designs.
- **Record name.** `spine-claim-v1-` followed by the lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 of `<accountID>:<bookID>`, as for designs.
- **Payload.** A `payload` field of type Bytes holds JSON: `version` (`1`), `deviceID` (a random ID that each install makes once and keeps in its user defaults), and `claimedAt`, with the same date encoding as cover choices.
- **Writes.** A device fetches the record, or starts a new one, and saves its own claim with `ifServerRecordUnchanged`, retrying up to three times on a conflict, like cover choices. It leaves the book to another device instead when the record holds that device's version-1 claim from the last 10 minutes (`SpineClaimV1.lifetime`), or when all three saves conflict. The record is never deleted: a claim stays in force after its design is saved, so a device that read the book before that save doesn't design it too, and the book's next claim saves over it.
- **Clocks.** `claimedAt` is the claiming device's time, and the checking device compares it with its own. A clock that is wrong by minutes ends a claim early or holds another device's failed book that much longer.
- **Failures.** A claim the device can't read or save for any other reason, such as without a network or before Production has the record type, counts as this device's, so the run designs the book. Claims only save requests: which design a book keeps is decided by the design's own save, as before.

### One Hardcover account in iCloud

Each person has one Hardcover account, and a device never mixes iCloud data from two accounts without asking (`CloudLibraryArchive.check(worklist:signedInAccountID:chosenAccountID:)`, `CloudSpineStore.checkAccounts()`). The Apple TV app follows the same rules.

- **Check.** Every reload, including those at launch, on activation, after a sign-in or sign-out, from **Sync now**, and from **Turn on iCloud storage**, and every generation run first reads the archive and the worklist; so does a worklist push while the app is in the foreground. Signed in, iCloud is clear unless a real account other than this device's, and not merged into it, has a Hardcover snapshot in the archive or names the worklist. The legacy `hardcover` placeholder never counts. Without a sign-in, iCloud is clear for the only library, which is the only real account with a snapshot and no `merges` entry, or for the library you chose. With several libraries and no choice, the app asks.
- **Paused.** Until iCloud is clear, the app reads and writes nothing in iCloud for any account. The library on screen keeps the designs, cover choices, series links, series looks, and Top Five pins that the device last loaded for it and keeps on the device, so they also show at launch while the check can't run, such as offline. That is only the signed-in account's library, or without a sign-in, the library the device last cleared or chose. While the prompt waits, a signed-in device shows its own copy of its library with those designs and choices, or without designs when none are on screen, such as right after signing in to another account, and the Hardcover sheet names the reason. Edits fail with the same reason. A due Hardcover download still runs and saves the copy on the device, but its iCloud read and upload fail and are dropped. Generation runs stop with the reason. A failed check changes nothing: paused iCloud stays paused, iCloud that an earlier check cleared stays clear, and the load reports the error.
- **Prompt.** An alert, "Another Hardcover account in iCloud", names the other accounts by `@username` from each account's newest snapshot that has one, else `account <ID>`, with **Merge** and **Keep iCloud Off**. Without a sign-in, "Choose a library" offers one button per library, named the same way.
- **Merge.** For each other account, newest first, so the newest account wins a shared book, the app copies that account's records for the books both libraries share, taking this account's books from the device's own copy of its library (`CloudSpineStore.copyRecords(from:into:books:)`): designs, cover choices, series links, the series looks of those books' look groups under either account's series link or none, and the Top Five pins of those books, in pin order. Each copy edits the JSON payload, so fields this app doesn't decode survive: it sets `accountID` to this account and gives a design this account's `workItemHash`. A copy only creates a record, so a record this account already has stays. Then the app adds each account to the archive's `merges`, mapped to this account, uploads this account's library when the archive has none, as when the upload after its download failed while the prompt waited, and reloads (`SpineStore.merge(_:into:)`). A failed copy or save records nothing and shows "Couldn't merge" with "Bookworms couldn't finish the merge. Try again later."; the prompt returns at the next check, and a repeat is safe. Without a downloaded library, the alert says "Your Hardcover library hasn't downloaded yet. Bookworms asks again when it has." The old account's records and snapshot stay in iCloud, and every device ignores them once the merge is recorded and this account's library is in iCloud; until then, a device without a sign-in still shows the old account's library. Books only the old account has don't appear.
- **Keep iCloud Off.** Sets `iCloudStorageOff` in the app's user defaults, so the device uses no iCloud data and doesn't ask again. An alert then explains how to delete Bookworms data from iCloud, which deletes all of it, this account's too: on the iPhone, Settings > your name > iCloud > Manage Account Storage > Bookworms; on the Mac, System Settings > your name > iCloud > Manage > Bookworms. **Turn on iCloud storage**, in the Hardcover sheet, removes the flag, checks again, and then uploads this account's library when the archive has none. Signing out keeps the flag.
- **Library choice.** Without a sign-in, the chosen account's ID is saved as `iCloudLibraryAccountID` in the app's user defaults. The app then shows and designs only that library, and a worklist of another account counts as none. A sign-in ignores the choice, and signing out keeps it.
- **`merges`.** An optional top-level object of the archive, from an old account ID to the account it merged into, absent until the first merge. Writers add entries and never remove one; when both sides hold a key, the stored value stays (`CloudLibraryArchive.recording(merges:into:)`). An account is merged into another when following `merges` from it reaches that account; the check stops at an account that repeats.

## Verification

Run the unit tests on a Simulator on the newest installed iOS runtime (`OS=latest`), since iPhone owners update quickly:

```sh
xcodebuild -project Bookworms.xcodeproj -scheme Bookworms \
  -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 18 Pro,OS=latest' -derivedDataPath DerivedData \
  OTHER_SWIFT_FLAGS='-disable-sandbox' test
```

Install the runtime that matches Xcode's SDK with `xcodebuild -downloadPlatform iOS`. The app targets iOS 27, so no iOS 26 runtime is needed.

To fit more Simulators in memory, slim one with [simslim](https://github.com/MobAI-App/simslim) (`brew install mobai-app/tap/simslim`) before testing on it: `simslim on <id>`. It reboots that Simulator with every simslim category of background daemons off; the setting lasts until `simslim off <id>` or an erase. The tests need none of those categories. A test that needs one, such as `icloud` or `siri`, should turn on only that one, with `--except <ids>` or `simslim enable <id> <ids>`. Run it only on a Simulator no other test run is using, because it reboots the Simulator. When `script/build_and_run.sh` has to boot a Simulator, it boots it slim with `icloud`, `siri`, and `photos` on for the app.

The tests use fictional books, except where the Discworld and House of Leaves rules need the real titles, fictional covers, and canned Hardcover responses, and need no iCloud account, network, Hardcover login, or model. The Hardcover Keychain test uses its own service name, and tests keep AI provider keys in the Keychain service `gay.ian.Bookworms.ai-provider.tests` and the backup model choice, its remembered rejection, and the providers' consent in the user defaults suite `gay.ian.Bookworms.tests`, so a test run never reads the keys or changes the settings that the app saved on the same Simulator. Simulator runs check rendering and the generation flow against the preview folder; the Simulator does not run background tasks. Check a signed device build for iCloud access, push delivery, the charging task, continued processing, and Private Cloud Compute quotas, and confirm that the Apple TV caches the designs.

### Pre-merge check

Run `python3 script/premerge.py` on a pull request's head commit before it merges, with the Bash sandbox off. The script prints one `PASS`, `FAIL`, or `SKIP` line per step and a last line for the whole run, writes all command output to `.local/premerge/<commit>/premerge.log`, and exits with 0 for a pass and 1 for a failure.

| Step | What passes |
|---|---|
| `tree` | The working tree has no uncommitted or untracked files, at the start and at the end, so the pass names one commit. The script runs `xcodegen generate`, so a `project.yml` change without its regenerated project fails here. |
| `lint` | `xcrun swift-format lint --strict` on the Swift files changed against the base, with the repository's `.swift-format`. |
| `build iPhone` | A Release build for the iPhone simulator, which compiles the code that Debug-only launch arguments leave out. |
| `build Mac` | A Release build for Apple silicon Macs, with the settings of `script/run_mac.sh`, unsigned. |
| `test` | The unit test classes the change touches (see below) on one slim iPhone simulator, the newest iPhone Pro on the newest installed iOS runtime, which the script creates, slims with `simslim on`, and deletes. The step fails without starting a simulator when ten are booted (four without simslim) or less than 15 percent of memory is free, and when simslim can't slim the new simulator. |
| `links` | Every relative link in the changed Markdown files leads to a file. |

A change that touches only Markdown files, `docs/`, the README's `screenshots/`, and `LICENSE` skips `lint`, both builds, and `test`. `--base BRANCH` compares with another branch than `origin/main`.

The `test` step runs only the classes that name the changed code, which the script's `tests` line lists, and the pass file records as `test_classes`. A class counts when its test file names a type that a changed app file declares or extends, before or after the change; a changed test file runs its own classes and the classes that name the helpers it declares, such as a stub store. Renamed files count as deleted and added, so a test still naming the old type runs. A change to the Desk's physics runs the Desk and 3D classes, for example, and skips `GenerationTests`, which takes most of the full suite's time. When no class names the changed code, such as for a view no test uses, the step only compiles the tests; a change to `script/` alone skips it. Every class runs with `--all-tests`, for a changed Swift file that declares no type, and for a change to anything else that builds into the app or its tests, such as `project.yml` or a resource, so each build-number change runs the full suite. The selection follows direct references only: a class that reaches the changed code through other code isn't selected, and the next full run catches what it breaks. Use `--all-tests` for a change to shared plumbing whose effects reach further than its names.

When every step passes, the script writes `premerge/<commit>.json` to the repository's shared Git folder (`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`), which every worktree uses. The workspace's merge gate, a `PreToolUse` hook on `Bash` (`.claude/hooks/merge-gate.py` in the workspace folder), refuses `gh pr merge` until the pull request's head commit has that file, so a new commit needs a new run. A branch that predates the script merges `main` first. The pass also lists the views the change touches (`script/visual-check.py --list`); when it lists any, the script's last line names the [visual check](#visual-check), which the gate doesn't require.

### Visual check

Before a change to how the app looks or moves merges, run `python3 script/visual-check.py`, with the Bash sandbox off. It maps the files changed against `origin/main` to views, builds the base commit and the head commit in Debug, and launches each on the iPhone and on the Mac. A change that touches no view prints `SKIP` and needs no check.

- **iPhone.** One slim simulator, the newest iPhone Pro on the newest installed iOS runtime, which the script creates and deletes. It turns off Device Hub's iOS simulator sound for the run and restores the setting afterward, loads the private preview library into each build with `script/load-preview-data.py --caches`, and starts no simulator when ten are booted (four without simslim) or less than 15 percent of memory is free.
- **Mac.** The Mac has no simulator. The script builds a Debug copy, signed ad hoc and without entitlements, with the scratch bundle ID `gay.ian.Bookworms.VisualCheck`, launches its executable with `CFFIXED_USER_HOME` set to a scratch home folder that holds the preview library, and photographs the copy's window with `screencapture -l`. The copy is a test copy, runs outside the sandbox, and never touches the installed app, its iCloud data, or its Keychain items; the script deletes the copy's preferences and saved state before and after each launch. The capture needs the Mac's lid open, its display awake, and Screen Recording allowed for the app that runs the script: with the lid closed, macOS has no active display and no window can be photographed ([what was tried](docs/investigations/mac-capture-lid-closed.md)), so the script skips the Mac launches; otherwise the bundle's brief lists the Mac launches as missing, and `--record pass` refuses the bundle. The window is inactive, so its accent colors show desaturated.

`RULES` in the script maps changed files to views, and `python3 script/visual-check.py --list` prints the views a change touches. A file that matches no rule still counts as a view when it is under `Views/` or `Support/`, or is a shader or an asset. `SHOTS` holds each view's launches for each platform; add a view's launches there. Desk and Dominoes launch with a fixed time frame (`-BookwormsPileFrame`), so both commits show the same books.

| View | iPhone launches | Mac launches |
|---|---|---|
| Library (`library`) | **Stack** in 3D with a glide to the seventh book, with a motion image, and **Shelf** | The same |
| Book details (`details`) | The first book's flight from **Stack** to its details, with a motion image | The same |
| Desk (`desk`) | The drop of 2025's books, their drop by month, and the gather, each with a motion image; the time frame Desk opens on; and a lifted book's details with the **Back** that returns it, with a motion image | The same |
| Dominoes (`dominoes`) | A run of August 2025's books, pushed three seconds after the chain stands, with a motion image | The same |
| Share images (`share`) | The Year in Books, Top Five, Desk, and Tall Stack images, each as the app writes it to `share-debug.png` | The Share section with Year in Books |
| Spines (`spines`) | The Spines tab, and its toolbar with no sign-in, with a rejected login, and with a failed sync | The Spines section |
| Account (`account`) | Share, Library's **Shelf**, and Desk, each with a rejected login's bar | Library's **Shelf** with the sidebar's account row: a username, a rejected login's mark, and no sign-in |
| Settings (`settings`) | The gear's **Settings** | The Settings window's **General** pane |
| The tabs without a library (`empty`), and unmapped drawing code | None; the script lists them as not captured | None |

Each launch saves a picture after a fixed wait. An iPhone launch with motion also records the simulator and keeps twelve frames as one image; the frames are spaced by how much the picture changes, so most show the motion and few show a loading screen, and the recording stays in the bundle at half size. A Mac launch with motion keeps eight window pictures taken at even intervals. Agents read images, not video. The script stops with an error when an iPhone launch saves no picture, and lists a Mac launch without one as missing. The bundle is `.local/visual-check/<commit>/`: the `-before`, `-after`, and side-by-side `-pair` images, the motion images, the iPhone recordings, `manifest.json`, and `REVIEW.md`. It shows the private preview library: never commit or publish it. The pair images are also the before-and-after sample sheet for the owner's styling approval, which this check doesn't replace.

The last line the script prints names the bundle's `REVIEW.md`, which lists the images, the commits, the changed files, and eight fixed questions drawn from the workspace rules: regressions, physical realism, legible lettering, the camera facing the action, motion, clipped or overlapping content, the Mac layout, and eyebrow labels. Start one agent on it, which may be a Sonnet agent, and have it follow that file and end with `VERDICT: pass` or `VERDICT: fail`. After a pass, run `python3 script/visual-check.py --record pass`, which writes `premerge/<commit>.visual.json` beside the pre-merge pass. After a fail, fix the regression and capture the new commit. `--record` needs the bundle of the checked-out commit. A bundle that lacks a platform's pictures, because the Mac's lid was closed or the run used `--platforms`, records a pass only with `--allow-missing`; the marker lists what was missing, and the session tells the owner which launches nobody looked at.

`--surfaces library,desk` captures the named views instead of the mapped ones, `--platforms iphone` or `--platforms mac` captures one platform, `--head COMMIT --base COMMIT` checks another change, such as a merged one, and `--keep-builds` keeps the builds in `.local/visual-check/build/` for another run.

The check can't judge taste, or how the app performs and feels on a real iPhone or Mac. A view that the script doesn't capture still needs a look by hand.

### Motion checks

Unit tests check that books move without jumps. Each check runs the code that places a book, projects the book's corners to screen points, and compares them with the helpers in `BookwormsTests/MotionCheck.swift`:

- `assertHandoff` compares the last frame of one phase or copy of a book with the first frame of the next. Every corner must stay within 0.5 points, and the shading, shadow, and veil within 0.02.
- `assertContinuous` samples a motion every 1e-4 of its progress, or every millisecond for a function of time, and fails where a corner's second difference passes 0.5 points. A jump keeps its size however fine the steps, while smooth motion's second difference shrinks with them.
- `assertSeam` checks that the velocity matches on both sides of a point where the code eases one part of a motion into the next.

A failed check attaches its samples as a CSV with the failing rows marked. The checks cover Library's flight in `ShelfLogicTests`: pulls and returns on **Shelf** and **Stack**, a pull from a nudged or peeked book, a return from the drift, and the end of a coast. They also cover the dominoes: the camera's takeover from **Follow** in `DominoCameraTests`, the setup's moves in `DominoSetupTests`, and the gathering in `DominoTests`. They don't cover rendering, physics, SwiftUI's animation engine, or what only the Mac draws; check those by video (see [Check a book's flight to its details](#check-a-books-flight-to-its-details)). Without a view, `withAnimation` runs its completion before it returns, so a check of `ShelfPullOut` reads the state an animation starts from as the next change begins (`beforeChange` in `ShelfLogicTests`).

Run them with the command above and `-only-testing:BookwormsTests/ShelfLogicTests -only-testing:BookwormsTests/DominoCameraTests -only-testing:BookwormsTests/DominoSetupTests -only-testing:BookwormsTests/DominoTests`.

To declare a deliberate discontinuity, such as a hop's takeoff, pass the samples beside it in `cuts` with a one-line comment that gives the reason, and check across it with `assertHandoff`, so the cut excuses only the change in speed.

Each open finding wraps the one assertion that shows it in `XCTExpectFailure`, which fails once the jump is gone, so its fix removes the expectation. Each expectation's message gives the jump's measured size:

| Transition | Cause |
|---|---|
| A **Shelf** pull of a book that the pointer has eased out | The flight scales the book about its center and shades it; the spine scales about its bottom edge and is flat. |
| A pull right after a peek (Space, then Return) | `ShelfPullOut.start(of:)` ignores `peekID`. |
| A pinch, a drag, or **Back to desk** that takes over from **Follow** | `PileCamera(matching:)` leaves out roll. |
| A book from Library that turns through 45 degrees while the drift sways it | `BookProjection.turn(by:)` switches the face it turns exactly. |

### Crash reports

Run `python3 script/crash-reports.py` with the Bash sandbox off, after `. ~/.appstoreconnect/bookworms.env`. It collects Bookworms crash reports from three places and prints a summary of each one it hasn't printed before: the time, the bundle ID, version and build, the device and OS, the exception, and the crashed thread's top frames.

| Source | What it reads |
|---|---|
| `mac` | `~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports` and its `Retired` folder on this Mac, which hold a report for every copy of the app. For a crash still in the system log, the summary adds Swift's `Fatal error` line, which the report leaves out. |
| `iphone` | Each paired iPhone's crash logs, copied with `xcrun devicectl` over the network. An iPhone that can't be reached is skipped with a note. |
| `testflight` | Crashes that a tester shared from TestFlight's crash prompt, for this app and the Apple TV app, through the App Store Connect API with the release key. Crashes nobody shared reach only Xcode's Organizer, which the API doesn't serve. |

The script never contacts an Apple TV, so it can run during TV quiet hours; Apple TV crashes arrive only through TestFlight. It copies every report to `.local/crash-reports/<source>/` and records the printed ones in `.local/crash-reports/seen.json`. `--all` prints every report again. It exits with 0 when nothing is new, 1 when it printed new reports, and 2 when a source failed.

## Credits

Spine fonts and Checkout Card's handwriting, Caveat, are open-source Google Fonts families (SIL Open Font License or Apache License), downloaded at runtime with their license files. The design models' readings choose among them with the classification, theme, and mood tags of the [Google Fonts repository](https://github.com/google/fonts/tree/main/tags) (`tags/all/families.csv`), which `script/spine-font-tags.py` copies into `Bookworms/SpineFontTags.json` for the atlas's families. The license of that tag data is unconfirmed; see [google/fonts issue #9347](https://github.com/google/fonts/issues/9347). The app icon is rendered from the Bookworms Apple TV repository's Blender model.

The desk stamp that restamps the Top Five tag is ["Wood Stamp 4"](https://sketchfab.com/3d-models/wood-stamp-4-396e3913472e43f3ba7664a5e61f7e41) by necmettinturkan, licensed under [CC BY 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/). The app draws stills baked from the model, of the stamp and its shadows (`StampStill`, `StampShadowNear`, `StampShadowFar`, from `script/stamp-still.py`), rather than the model itself; the bake makes its varnish, rubber, and brass rougher and less glossy.

Tall Stack's figures (`Bookworms/Figures`) come from [Poly Pizza](https://poly.pizza): the [house](https://poly.pizza/m/sGgL4Nt7I7) by Kenney, the [windmill](https://poly.pizza/m/jpHoi9xDLG) and the [stone tower](https://poly.pizza/m/Rz4U5dZJz8) by Quaternius, and the [lighthouse](https://poly.pizza/m/KRebJXIRb8) by MaverickFX, under CC0; and the [cat](https://poly.pizza/m/6dM1J6f6pm9), [elephant](https://poly.pizza/m/cx0-TiCjDOx), and [giraffe](https://poly.pizza/m/80w8kwQU0QH) by Poly by Google and the [phone booth](https://poly.pizza/m/nx9Cqdzq97) by Zsky, under [CC BY 3.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/3.0/). Blender converted each from glTF to USDZ unchanged. The mannequin is burning_barb's [Prototyping Mannequin](https://burning-barb.itch.io/mannequin), under [CC0](https://creativecommons.org/publicdomain/zero/1.0/); `script/figure-reader.py` lowers its arms from the T-pose, colors it as beech with darker joints, splits its head into a `head` mesh that the app finds to hold the photo in front of, and exports `figure-reader.usdz` with Blender: `blender -b mannequin.blend --python script/figure-reader.py -- 84 <output stem>`, where 84 is the arms' angle in degrees.

The pile's table (`TableWood`) is [White Oak Veneer](https://polyhaven.com/a/white_oak_veneer) by Jenelle van Heerden from Poly Haven, under [CC0](https://polyhaven.com/license). The bundled file is the 2K diffuse map scaled to 1024 pixels; the app tints it a darker brown, the Shelf's bookcase darkens it to walnut, and the walnut wall's grain (`WallGrain`) is baked from it. The room's light, the page edges' texture, and the impact sounds are generated in code.

The Mac's **Bookworms > About Bookworms** and the iPhone's **Settings > Credits** show these attributions from `Bookworms/Credits.rtf`. No font file ships in the app: the font atlas holds only small rendered images of the families' letters (`Bookworms/SpineFontAtlas.lzfse`).
